1.

The determinant
\tau_{m}^{(n)}(z)
can be written as the Wronskian

\tau_{m}^{(n)}(z)=(-1)^{m(m-1)/2}\Wr\big(p^{(n-m+1)}_{1},p^{(n-m+1)}_{3},\ldots,p^{(n-m+1)}_{2m-1}\big),

(5.4)

with
p^{(n)}_{k}(z)=L^{(n)}_{k}(\tfrac{1}{2}z)
,
and is related to the generalised Umemura polynomial by

U_{m,0}^{(n-m+1)}(z)=U_{0,m}^{(-n-m-1)}(z)=c_{0,m}(-1)^{m(m+1)/2}\left(\tfrac{1}{2}\right)^{m(m-1)/2}\tau_{m}^{(n)}(z).

2.

The function
q_{n}(z;m)
satisfies equation (
4.10
)
with

(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=\big(\tfrac{1}{8}(m-n)^{2},-\tfrac{1}{8}(m+n+2)^{2},-m-1\big).

5.1
Rational solutions in terms of generalised Laguerre polynomials

Each of the transformations
\mathcal{R}_{1}
(
3.3
),
\mathcal{R}_{2}
(
3.13
) and
\mathcal{R}_{3}
(
3.24
) is actually four transformations since there are two independent choices of sign for
a=\pm\sqrt{2\alpha}
and
b=\pm\sqrt{2\beta}
. This can lead to four different solutions from applying the transformation to a given solution. This is illustrated in Table
5.1
which shows the effect on the parameters of applying the transformations
\mathcal{R}_{1}
(
3.3
) and
\mathcal{R}_{1}^{2}
on a solution
w(z)
of
P
V
(
1.2
) with
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=\big(\tfrac{1}{2}a^{2},-\tfrac{1}{2}b^{2},c\big)
; see also Example
5.3
.

\begin{array}[]{|c|ccc|}\hline\cr&\alpha_{1}&\beta_{1}&\gamma_{1}\\
\hline\cr\mathcal{R}_{1}(a,b,c)&\tfrac{1}{8}(a-b+c+1)^{2}&-\tfrac{1}{8}(a-b-c+1)^{2}&a+b\\
\mathcal{R}_{1}(-a,b,c)&\tfrac{1}{8}(a+b-c-1)^{2}&-\tfrac{1}{8}(a+b+c-1)^{2}&-a+b\\
\mathcal{R}_{1}(a,-b,c)&\tfrac{1}{8}(a+b+c+1)^{2}&-\tfrac{1}{8}(a+b-c+1)^{2}&a-b\\
\mathcal{R}_{1}(-a,-b,c)&\tfrac{1}{8}(a-b-c-1)^{2}&-\tfrac{1}{8}(a-b+c-1)^{2}&-a-b\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr\mathcal{R}_{1}^{2}(a,b,c)&\tfrac{1}{2}(a+1)^{2}&-\tfrac{1}{2}(b-1)^{2}&c\\
\mathcal{R}_{1}^{2}(-a,b,c)&\tfrac{1}{2}(a-1)^{2}&-\tfrac{1}{2}(b-1)^{2}&c\\
\mathcal{R}_{1}^{2}(a,-b,c)&\tfrac{1}{2}(a+1)^{2}&-\tfrac{1}{2}(b+1)^{2}&c\\
\mathcal{R}_{1}^{2}(-a,-b,c)&\tfrac{1}{2}(a-1)^{2}&-\tfrac{1}{2}(b+1)^{2}&c\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

Table 5.1:
The effect on the parameters of applying the transformations
\mathcal{R}_{1}
(
3.3
) and
\mathcal{R}_{1}^{2}
on a solution
w(z)
of
P
V
with
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=\big(\tfrac{1}{2}a^{2},-\tfrac{1}{2}b^{2},c\big)
.

5.1.1
First discrete equation: asymmetric discrete
P
II

For the Bäcklund transformation
\mathcal{R}_{1}=\mathcal{T}_{1,-1,1}
, we know that
\mathcal{R}_{1}^{2}(a,b,c)=(a+1,b-1,c)
, so that

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}^{2}\big(w_{m,n}^{(\mu)};m,m+2n+1+\mu,\mu\big)

\displaystyle=\big(w_{m+1,n-1}^{(\mu)};m+1,m+2n+\mu,\mu\big),

(5.5a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}^{2}\big(w_{m,n}^{(\mu)};-m,m+2n+1+\mu,\mu\big)

\displaystyle=\big(w_{m-1,n}^{(\mu)};1-m,m+2n+\mu,\mu\big),

(5.5b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}^{2}\big(w_{m,n}^{(\mu)};m,-m-2n-1-\mu,\mu\big)

\displaystyle=\big(w_{m+1,n}^{(\mu)};m+1,-m-2n-2-\mu,\mu\big),

(5.5c)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}^{2}\big(w_{m,n}^{(\mu)};-m,-m-2n-1-\mu,\mu\big)

\displaystyle=\big(w_{m-1,n+1}^{(\mu)};1-m,-m-2n-2-\mu,\mu\big).

(5.5d)

From (
5.5
) we see that except in the case (
5.5c
), the Bäcklund transformation
\mathcal{R}_{1}
(
3.3
) can only be applied a finite number of times before the sequence terminates as either
m
or
n
becomes zero, as illustrated in the following example.

Example 5.3
.

Consider the rational solution

\displaystyle w_{1,1}^{(\mu-3)}(z)

\displaystyle=-\frac{(z-\mu+1)\big[z^{2}-2\mu z+\mu(\mu+1)\big]}{z^{2}-2\mu z+\mu(\mu-1)},

which satisfies
P
V
(
1.2
) with
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=\big(\tfrac{1}{2},-\tfrac{1}{2}(\mu+1)^{2},\mu-3\big)
, so that

q_{0}(z)=\frac{w_{1,1}^{(\mu-3/2)}(z)+1}{w_{1,1}^{(\mu-3/2)}(z)-1}=1+\frac{2}{z-\mu}-\frac{4(z-\mu+1)}{z^{2}-2\mu z+\mu(\mu-1)}.

Applying the Bäcklund transformation
\mathcal{R}_{1}
(
3.3
), with
(a,b,c)=(1,\mu+1,\mu-3)
,
gives the sequence of rational solutions

\displaystyle q_{1}^{[a]}(z)

\displaystyle=1-\frac{2}{z-\mu+1}+\frac{4(z-\mu)}{z^{2}-2\mu z+\mu(\mu-1)},

\displaystyle q_{2}^{[a]}(z)

\displaystyle=1-\frac{4(z-\mu+1)}{z^{2}-2(\mu-1)z+(\mu-1)(\mu-2)},\qquad q_{3}^{[a]}(z)=1.

Applying
\mathcal{R}_{1}
(
3.3
), with

(a,b,c)=(-1,\mu+1,\mu-3)
, gives the sequence of rational solutions

q_{1}^{[b]}(z)=-1+\frac{2\mu}{z}-\frac{2}{z-\mu+1},\qquad q_{2}^{[b]}(z)=1,

and applying
\mathcal{R}_{1}
(
3.3
), with

(a,b,c)=(-1,-\mu-1,\mu-3)
, gives the sequence of rational solutions

q_{1}^{[d]}(z)=-1-\frac{4(z-\mu)}{z^{2}-2\mu z+\mu(\mu+1)},\qquad q_{2}^{[d]}(z)=1.

In each case the Bäcklund transformation only produces a finite number of solutions.

Applying
\mathcal{R}_{1}
(
3.3
), with

(a,b,c)=(1,-\mu-1,\mu-3)
, gives an infinite sequence of solutions.
The next few are

\displaystyle q_{1}(z)

\displaystyle=1-\frac{2\mu}{z}+2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{z^{2}-2\mu z+\mu(\mu-1)}{z^{2}-2\mu z+\mu(\mu+1)},

\displaystyle q_{2}(z)

\displaystyle=1+2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{z^{2}-2(\mu+1)z+\mu(\mu+1)}{z^{4}-4\mu z^{3}+6\mu^{2}z^{2}-4\mu(\mu^{2}-1)z+\mu^{2}(\mu^{2}-1)},

\displaystyle q_{3}(z)

\displaystyle=1-\frac{2\mu}{z}+2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{z^{3}-3(\mu+1)z^{2}+3\mu(\mu+1)z-\mu(\mu^{2}-1)}{z^{4}-4(\mu+1)z^{3}+6(\mu+1)^{2}z^{2}-4\mu(\mu+1)(\mu+2)z+\mu(\mu+1)^{2}(\mu+2)}.

Generally for
n\geq 0

\displaystyle q_{2n}(z)=1+2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{T_{n,1}^{(\mu-2)}(z)}{T_{n,2}^{(\mu-4)}(z)},\qquad q_{2n+1}(z)=1-\frac{2\mu}{z}+2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{T_{n+1,1}^{(\mu-3)}(z)}{T_{n,2}^{(\mu-3)}(z)},

satisfy

q_{n+1}+q_{n-1}=\frac{4}{z}\,\frac{(n+\mu+2)q_{n}-\tfrac{3}{2}-(\mu-\tfrac{3}{2})(-1)^{n}}{1-q_{n}^{2}},

which is asymmetric d
P
II
(
3.7
) with
\lambda=\mu+2
,
\rho=-\tfrac{3}{2}
and
\varphi=-\mu+\tfrac{3}{2}
, whilst
x_{n}=q_{2n}
and
y_{n}=q_{2n+1}
satisfy

\displaystyle x_{n+1}+x_{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{z}\,\frac{(2n+\mu+3)y_{n}+\mu-3}{1-y_{n}^{2}},\qquad y_{n}+y_{n-1}=\frac{4}{z}\,\frac{(2n+\mu+2)x_{n}-\mu}{1-x_{n}^{2}}.

Also, applying the inverse transformation
\mathcal{R}_{1}^{-1}=\mathcal{T}_{-1,1,-1}
we obtain

q_{-1}(z)=1-\frac{2\mu}{z}+\frac{2}{z-\mu+1},\qquad q_{-2}(z)=1.

We note that

\mathcal{Q}_{1}^{3}\left(q_{1,1}^{(\mu)};1,\mu+1,\mu-3\right)=1,\qquad\mathcal{Q}_{1}^{2}\left(q_{1,1}^{(\mu)};-1,\mu+1,\mu-3\right)=1,\qquad\mathcal{Q}_{1}^{2}\left(q_{1,1}^{(\mu)};-1,-\mu-1,\mu-3\right)=1.

where
\mathcal{Q}_{1}
is the Bäcklund transformation

\mathcal{Q}_{1}(q;a,b,c)=\left(\frac{2}{z(q^{2}-1)}\left\{{z}\frac{\mathrm{d}{q}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}-(a-b)q-a-b\right\};\tfrac{1}{2}(a-b+c+1),\tfrac{1}{2}(-a+b+c-1),a+b\right).

Further if
\mathcal{R}_{1}
(
3.3
) is the Bäcklund transformation given by (
5.5c
) then

w_{1,1}^{(\mu-3)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\frac{1}{{v}_{1,1}^{(\mu)}(z)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip w_{2,1}^{(\mu-3)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\frac{1}{{v}_{2,1}^{(\mu)}(z)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip w_{3,1}^{(\mu-3)}(z)

More generally, using the solution
w_{m,n}^{(\mu)}(z)
, for fixed
m
and
n
, then we have the following Lemma.

Lemma 5.4
.

Consider the rational functions

Q_{2N}(z)=X_{N}(z)=\frac{w_{N+m,n}^{(\mu)}(z)+1}{w_{N+m,n}^{(\mu)}(z)-1},\qquad Q_{2N+1}(z)=Y_{N}(z)=\frac{1+v_{N+m,n}^{(\mu+2n+1)}(z)}{1-v_{N+m,n}^{(\mu+2n+1)}(z)},

with
w_{m,n}^{(\mu)}(z)
and
v_{m,n}^{(\mu)}(z)
as given in Theorem
4.4
,
then
Q_{N}(z)
satisfies

Q_{N+1}+Q_{N-1}=\frac{4}{z}\frac{(N+2m+2n+\mu+1)Q_{N}-n-\tfrac{1}{2}-(n+\mu+\tfrac{1}{2})(-1)^{N}}{1-Q_{N}^{2}},

which is asymmetric
d
P
II
(
3.7
) with
\lambda=2m+2n+\mu+1
,
\rho=-n-\tfrac{1}{2}
and
\varphi=-n-\mu-\tfrac{1}{2}
,
whilst
X_{N}(z)
and
Y_{N}(z)
satisfy the discrete system

\displaystyle X_{N+1}+X_{N}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{z}\frac{(2N+2m+2n+\mu+2)Y_{N}+\mu}{1-Y_{N}^{2}},

(5.6a)

\displaystyle Y_{N}+Y_{N-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{z}\frac{(2N+2m+2n+\mu+1)X_{N}-2n-\mu-1}{1-X_{N}^{2}}.

(5.6b)

Proof.

From the transformations

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}\big(w_{m,n}^{(\mu)};m,-m-2n-1-\mu,\mu\big)

\displaystyle=\big({1/{v}_{m,n}^{(\mu+2n+1)}};m+n+1+\mu,-m-n-1,-\mu-2n-1\big),

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}\big(v_{m,n}^{(\mu)};m+n+1,-m+n-\mu,\mu\big)

\displaystyle=\big({1/{w}_{m+1,n}^{(\mu-2n-1)}};m+\mu+1,-m-1,-\mu+2n+1\big),

we obtain the sequence of solutions

w_{m,n}^{(\mu)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\frac{1}{{v}_{m,n}^{(\mu+2n+1)}}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip w_{m+1,n}^{(\mu)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\frac{1}{{v}_{m+1,n}^{(\mu+2n+1)}}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip w_{m+2,n}^{(\mu)}

from which the result follows.
We note that

\mathcal{R}_{1}^{2}\Big(w_{m,n}^{(\mu)}\Big)=w_{m+1,n}^{(\mu)},\qquad\mathcal{R}_{1}^{2}\Big(1/{v}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}\Big)=1/{v}_{m+1,n}^{(\mu)}.

∎

Remarks 5.5
.

1.

An equivalent result can be obtained using the inverse transformation
\mathcal{R}_{1}^{-1}=\mathcal{T}_{-1,1,-1}
since

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}^{-1}\big(w_{m,n}^{(\mu)};-m,m+2n+1+\mu,\mu\big)

\displaystyle=\big(v_{m,n}^{(\mu+2n+1)};-m-n-1,m+n+1+\mu,2n+1+\mu\big),

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}^{-1}\big(v_{m,n}^{(\mu)};-m-n-1,m-n+\mu,\mu\big)

\displaystyle=\big(w_{m+1,n}^{(\mu-2n-1)};-m-1,m+1+\mu,-2n-1+\mu\big).

2.

We note that if
\mu=-n-\tfrac{1}{2}
then

Q_{2N}(z)=\frac{w_{N+m,n}^{(-n-1/2)}(z)+1}{w_{N+m,n}^{(-n-1/2)}(z)-1},\qquad Q_{2N+1}(z)=\frac{v_{N+m,n}^{(n+1/2)}(z)+1}{v_{N+m,n}^{(n+1/2)}(z-1},

are solutions to

Q_{N+1}+Q_{N-1}=\frac{4}{z}\frac{(N+2m+n+\tfrac{1}{2})Q_{N}+n+\tfrac{1}{2}}{1-Q_{N}^{2}},

which is d
P
II
(
1.7
) with
\zeta=4/z
,
\lambda=2m+n+\tfrac{1}{2}
and
\rho=n+\tfrac{1}{2}
.
Hence, although there is no alternating term, the structure of the even and odd terms is different.

Alternatively, using the solution
v_{m,n}^{(\mu)}(z)
, for fixed
m
and
n
, then we have the following Lemma.

Lemma 5.6
.

Consider the rational functions

Q_{2N}(z)=X_{N}(z)=\frac{v_{m,N+n}^{(\mu)}+1}{v_{m,N+n}^{(\mu)}-1},\qquad Q_{2N+1}(z)=Y_{N}(z)=\frac{u_{N+n+1,m}^{(\mu+2m+1)}+1}{u_{N+n+1,m}^{(\mu+2m+1)}-1},

with
v_{m,n}^{(\mu)}(z)
and
u_{m,n}^{(\mu)}(z)
as given in Theorem
4.4
,
then
Q_{N}(z)
satisfies

Q_{N+1}+Q_{N-1}=\frac{4}{z}\frac{(N+2n+1-\mu)Q_{N}+\mu+(m+\tfrac{1}{2})[1+(-1)^{N}]}{1-Q_{N}^{2}},

which is asymmetric
d
P
II
(
3.7
) with
\lambda=2n+1-\mu
,
\rho=m+\tfrac{1}{2}+\mu
and
\varphi=m+\tfrac{1}{2}
,
whilst
X_{N}(z)
and
Y_{N}(z)
satisfy the discrete system

\displaystyle X_{N+1}+X_{N}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{z}\frac{(2N+2n+2-\mu)Y_{N}+\mu}{1-Y_{N}^{2}},

(5.7a)

\displaystyle Y_{N}+Y_{N-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{z}\frac{(2N+2n+1-\mu)X_{N}+\mu+2m+1}{1-X_{N}^{2}}.

(5.7b)

Proof.

From the transformations

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}\big(v_{m,n}^{(\mu)};m+n+1,m-n+\mu,\mu\big)

\displaystyle=\big(u_{n+1,m}^{(\mu+2m+1)};n+1,-n-1-\mu,2m+1+\mu\big),

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}\big(u_{m,n}^{(\mu)};m,-m-2n-1+\mu,\mu\big)

\displaystyle=\big(v_{n,m}^{(\mu)};m+n+1,-m-n-1+\mu,-2n-1+\mu\big)

we obtain the sequence of solutions

v_{m,n}^{(\mu)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip u_{n+1,m}^{(\mu+2m+1)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip v_{m,n+1}^{(\mu)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip u_{n+2,m}^{(\mu+2m+1)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip v_{m,n+2}^{(\mu)}

from which the result follows. We note that

\mathcal{R}_{1}^{2}\Big(v_{m,n}^{(\mu)}\Big)=w_{m,n+1}^{(\mu)},\qquad\mathcal{R}_{1}^{2}\Big(u_{n,m}^{(\mu)}\Big)=u_{n+1,m}^{(\mu)}.

∎

5.1.2
Second discrete equation

The effect on the parameters of applying the transformations
\mathcal{R}_{2}
(
3.13
) and
\mathcal{R}_{2}^{2}
on a solution
w(z)
of
P
V
with
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=\big(\tfrac{1}{2}a^{2},-\tfrac{1}{2}b^{2},c\big)
is given in Table
5.2
.

\begin{array}[]{|c|ccc|}\hline\cr&\alpha_{1}&\beta_{1}&\gamma_{1}\\
\hline\cr\mathcal{R}_{2}(a,b,c)&\tfrac{1}{8}(a+b-c-1)^{2}&-\tfrac{1}{8}(a+b+c+1)^{2}&-a+b+1\\
\mathcal{R}_{2}(-a,b,c)&\tfrac{1}{8}(a-b+c+1)^{2}&-\tfrac{1}{8}(a-b-c-1)^{2}&a+b+1\\
\mathcal{R}_{2}(a,-b,c)&\tfrac{1}{8}(a-b-c-1)^{2}&-\tfrac{1}{8}(a-b+c+1)^{2}&-a-b+1\\
\mathcal{R}_{2}(-a,-b,c)&\tfrac{1}{8}(a+b+c+1)^{2}&-\tfrac{1}{8}(a+b-c-1)^{2}&a-b+1\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr\mathcal{R}_{2}^{2}(a,b,c)&\tfrac{1}{2}(a-1)^{2}&-\tfrac{1}{2}(b+1)^{2}&c+2\\
\mathcal{R}_{2}^{2}(-a,b,c)&\tfrac{1}{2}(a+1)^{2}&-\tfrac{1}{2}(b+1)^{2}&c+2\\
\mathcal{R}_{2}^{2}(a,-b,c)&\tfrac{1}{2}(a-1)^{2}&-\tfrac{1}{2}(b-1)^{2}&c+2\\
\mathcal{R}_{2}^{2}(-a,-b,c)&\tfrac{1}{2}(a+1)^{2}&-\tfrac{1}{2}(b-1)^{2}&c+2\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

Table 5.2:
The effect on the parameters of applying the transformations
\mathcal{R}_{2}
(
3.13
) and
\mathcal{R}_{2}^{2}
on a solution
w(z)
of
P
V
with
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=\big(\tfrac{1}{2}a^{2},-\tfrac{1}{2}b^{2},c\big)
.

In the following example, we illustrate how using the Bäcklund transformation
\mathcal{R}_{2}
(
3.13
) gives rise to a hierarchy of solutions of (
3.18
) in terms of generalised Laguerre polynomials.

Example 5.7
.

Applying the Bäcklund transformation
\mathcal{R}_{2}
(
3.13
), with
(a,b,c)=(-3,\mu,\mu)
, to the solution

v_{1,1}^{(\mu)}(z)=1-\frac{z\big[z^{2}-2\mu z+\mu(\mu+1)\big]\big[z^{2}-2\mu z+\mu(\mu-1)\big]}{(z-\mu)\big[z^{4}-4\mu z^{3}+6\mu^{2}z^{2}-4\mu(\mu^{2}-1)z+\mu^{2}(\mu^{2}-1)\big]},

we obtain the sequence of rational solutions of
P
V
(
1.2
)

v_{1,1}^{(\mu)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{2}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip u_{2,1}^{(\mu+4)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip v_{1,2}^{(\mu+2)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{2}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip u_{3,1}^{(\mu+6)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{2}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip v_{1,3}^{(\mu+4)}

.
Hence we obtain the solutions of equation (
3.18
)

\displaystyle q_{0}(z)

\displaystyle=-1+\frac{2\mu}{z}+\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{z^{2}-2\mu+\mu(\mu+1)}{z^{2}-2\mu+\mu(\mu-1)},

\displaystyle q_{1}(z)

\displaystyle=1+2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{z^{4}-4(\mu+1)z^{3}+6(\mu+1)^{2}z^{2}-4\mu(\mu+1)(\mu+2)z+\mu(\mu+1)^{2}(\mu+2)}{z^{2}-2\mu+\mu(\mu+1)}.

Consequently, if we define the rational functions

\displaystyle q_{2n}(z)

\displaystyle=x_{n}(z)=\frac{v_{1,n+1}^{(2n+\mu)}(z)+1}{v_{1,n+1}^{(2n+\mu)}(z)-1}=-1+\frac{2(\mu+2n)}{z}+2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{T_{0,n+2}^{(\mu-3)}}{T_{1,n+1}^{(\mu-3)}(z)},

\displaystyle q_{2n+1}(z)

\displaystyle=y_{n}(z)=\frac{u_{n+2,1}^{(2n+\mu+4)}(z)+1}{u_{n+2,1}^{(2n+\mu+4)}(z)-1}=1+2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{T_{1,n+2}^{(\mu-3)}}{T_{0,n+2}^{(\mu-3)}(z)},

then
q_{n}(z)
satisfies

\frac{n+\mu+2}{q_{n+1}+q_{n}}+\frac{n+\mu+1}{q_{n}+q_{n-1}}=\tfrac{1}{2}z-\frac{\big\{n+\mu+\tfrac{3}{2}[1+(-1)^{n}]\big\}q_{n}+3-\mu}{1-q_{n}^{2}},

which is equation (
3.18
) with
\lambda=\mu+\tfrac{3}{2}
,
\rho=3-\mu
and
\varphi=\tfrac{3}{2}
,
whilst
x_{n}(z)
and
y_{n}(z)
satisfy the discrete system

\displaystyle\frac{2n+\mu+3}{x_{n+1}+y_{n}}+\frac{2n+\mu+2}{x_{n}+y_{n}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}z-\frac{(2n+\mu+1)y_{n}+3-\mu}{1-y_{n}^{2}},

\displaystyle\frac{2n+\mu+2}{x_{n}+y_{n}}+\frac{2n+\mu+1}{x_{n}+y_{n-1}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}z-\frac{(2n+\mu+3)x_{n}+3-\mu}{1-x_{n}^{2}}.

More generally, using the solution
v_{m,n}^{(\mu)}(z)
, for fixed
m
and
n
, then we have the following Lemma.

Lemma 5.8
.

Consider the rational functions

Q_{2N}(z)=X_{N}(z)=\frac{v_{m,N+n}^{(2N+\mu)}(z)+1}{v_{m,N+n}^{(2N+\mu)}(z)-1},\qquad Q_{2N+1}(z)=Y_{N}(z)=\frac{u_{N+n+1,m}^{(2N+\mu+2m+2)}(z)+1}{u_{N+n+1,m}^{(2N+\mu+2m+2)}(z)-1},

with
v_{m,n}^{(\mu)}(z)
and
u_{m,n}^{(\mu)}(z)
as given in Theorem
4.4
,
then
Q_{N}(z)
satisfies

\frac{N+m+\mu+1}{Q_{N+1}+Q_{N}}+\frac{N+m+\mu}{Q_{N}+Q_{N-1}}=\tfrac{1}{2}z-\frac{\big\{N+[1+(-1)^{N}](m+\tfrac{1}{2})+\mu\big\}Q_{N}+2n+1-\mu}{1-Q_{N}^{2}},

which is equation (
3.18
) with
\lambda=m+\mu-\tfrac{1}{2}
,
\rho=2n-\mu+1
and
\varphi=m+\tfrac{1}{2}
,
whilst
X_{N}(z)
and
Y_{N}(z)
satisfy the discrete system

\displaystyle\frac{2N+m+\mu+2}{X_{N+1}+Y_{N}}+\frac{2N+m+\mu+1}{X_{N}+Y_{N}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}z-\frac{(2N+\mu+1)Y_{N}+2n+1-\mu}{1-Y_{N}^{2}},

(5.8a)

\displaystyle\frac{2N+m+\mu+1}{X_{N}+Y_{N}}+\frac{2N+m+\mu}{X_{N}+Y_{N-1}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}z-\frac{(2N+2m+\mu+1)X_{N}+2n+1-\mu}{1-X_{N}^{2}}.

(5.8b)

Proof.

From the transformations

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{2}\big(v_{m,n}^{(\mu)};-m-n-1,m-n+\mu,\mu\big)

\displaystyle=\big(u_{n+1,m}^{(\mu+2m+2)};-n-1,-n+\mu,2m+2+\mu\big),

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{2}\big(u_{n,m}^{(\mu+2m+2)};-n,-n+1+\mu,2m+2+\mu\big)

\displaystyle=\big(v_{m,n}^{(\mu+2)};m+n+1,-m-n-1+\mu,2m-1+\mu\big),

we obtain the sequence of solutions

v_{m,n}^{(\mu)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{2}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip u_{n+1,m}^{(\mu+2m+2)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{2}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip v_{m,n+1}^{(\mu+2)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{2}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip u_{n+2,m}^{(\mu+2m+4)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{2}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip v_{m,n+2}^{(\mu+4)}

from which the result follows.
We note that

\mathcal{R}_{1}^{2}\Big(v_{m,n}^{(\mu)}\Big)=v_{m,n+1}^{(\mu+2)},\qquad\mathcal{R}_{1}^{2}\Big(u_{n,m}^{(\mu)}\Big)=u_{n+1,m}^{(\mu+2)}.

∎

Alternatively, using the solution
w_{m,n}^{(\mu)}(z)
, for fixed
m
and
n
, then we have the following Lemma.

Lemma 5.9
.

Consider the rational functions

Q_{2N}(z)=X_{N}(z)=\frac{w_{N+m,n}^{(\mu-2N)}(z)+1}{w_{N+m,n}^{(\mu-2N)}(z)-1},\qquad Q_{2N+1}(z)=Y_{N}(z)=\frac{v_{N+m,n}^{(\mu+2n-2N)}(z)+1}{v_{N+m,n}^{(\mu+2n-2N)}(z)-1},

with
w_{m,n}^{(\mu)}(z)
and
v_{m,n}^{(\mu)}(z)
as given in Theorem
4.4
,
then
Q_{N}(z)
satisfies

\frac{N-n-\mu}{Q_{N+1}+Q_{N}}+\frac{N-n-\mu-1}{Q_{N}+Q_{N-1}}+\tfrac{1}{2}z+\frac{\big\{N-\mu-(n+\tfrac{1}{2})[1+(-1)^{N}]\big\}Q_{N}+2m+2n+1+\mu}{1-Q_{N}^{2}}=0,

which is equation (
3.18
) with
\lambda=-n-\mu-\tfrac{1}{2}
,
\rho=2m+2n+\mu+1
and
\varphi=-n-\tfrac{1}{2}
,
whilst
X_{N}(z)
and
Y_{N}(z)
satisfy the discrete system

\displaystyle\frac{2N-n-\mu+1}{X_{N+1}+Y_{N}}+\frac{2N-n-\mu}{X_{N}+Y_{N}}+\tfrac{1}{2}z+\frac{(2N-\mu+1)Y_{N}+2m+2n+1+\mu}{1-Y_{N}^{2}}=0,

(5.9a)

\displaystyle\frac{2N-n-\mu}{X_{N}+Y_{N}}+\frac{2N-n-\mu-1}{X_{N}+Y_{N-1}}+\tfrac{1}{2}z+\frac{(2N-2n-\mu-1)X_{N}+2m+2n+1+\mu}{1-X_{N}^{2}}=0.

(5.9b)

Proof.

From the transformations

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{2}^{-1}\big(w_{m,n}^{(\mu)};m,m+2n+1+\mu,\mu\big)

\displaystyle=\big(v_{m,n}^{(\mu+2n)};m+n+1,m+n+\mu,2n+\mu\big),

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{2}^{-1}\big(v_{m,n}^{(\mu+2n)};m+n+1,m+n+\mu,2n+\mu\big)

\displaystyle=\big(w_{m+1,n}^{(\mu-2)};m,m+2n+1+\mu,\mu\big)

we obtain the sequence of solutions

w_{m,n}^{(\mu)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{2}^{-1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip v_{m,n}^{(\mu+2n)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{2}^{-1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip w_{m+1,n}^{(\mu-2)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{2}^{-1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip v_{m,n}^{(\mu+2n-2)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{2}^{-1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip w_{m+2,n}^{(\mu-4)}

from which the result follows.
∎

Remark 5.10
.

From the transformations

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{2}^{-1}\big(w_{m,n}^{(\mu)};m,-m-2n-1-\mu,\mu\big)

\displaystyle=\big(1/{u}_{n,m+1}^{(\mu+2m+2n+2n)};-n-k,-n-1,-2m-2n-1-\mu\big),

\displaystyle=\big(w_{m+1,n}^{(\mu-2m-2n-2)};m+1,m-\mu,-2m-2n-2+\mu\big),

we obtain the sequence of solutions

w_{m,n}^{(\mu)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{2}^{-1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\frac{1}{{u}_{n,m+1}^{(\mu+2m+2n+2)}}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{2}^{-1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip w_{m+1,n+1}^{(\mu-2)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{2}^{-1}\\[-8.0pt]

-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip w_{m+2,n+2}^{(\mu-4)}.

Example 5.11
.

Applying the Bäcklund transformation
\mathcal{R}_{3}
(

), with

, to the solution

\frac{1}{{w}_{1,1}^{(\mu-3)}(z)}=-\frac{z^{2}-2\mu z+\mu(\mu-1)}{(z-\mu+1)\big[z^{2}-2\mu z+\mu(\mu+1)\big]},

we obtain the sequence of rational solutions of

(

)

\frac{1}{{w}_{1,1}^{(\mu-3)}}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{3}\\[-8.0pt]

so that the effect of applying
\mathcal{R}_{3}
three times is

\mathcal{R}_{3}^{3}\Big(1/{{w}_{1,1}^{(\mu-3)}}\Big)=1/{{w}_{1,1}^{(\mu-4)}},\qquad\mathcal{R}_{3}^{3}\Big({v}_{1,1}^{(\mu)}\Big)={v}_{1,2}^{(\mu+1)},\qquad\mathcal{R}_{3}^{3}\Big({u}_{2,1}^{(\mu+3)}\Big)={u}_{3,1}^{(\mu+4)}.

Hence we obtain the rational solutions of the ternary d
P
I
(
3.28
)

\displaystyle x_{0}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{w_{1,1}^{(\mu-3)}(z)}{1-w_{1,1}^{(\mu-3)}(z)}=-1+\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{z^{2}-2(\mu-1)z+\mu(\mu-1)}{z-\mu},

\displaystyle x_{1}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{v_{1,1}^{(\mu)}(z)-1}=-1+\frac{\mu}{z}+\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{z^{2}-2\mu z+\mu(\mu-1)}{z^{2}-2\mu z+\mu(\mu+1)},

\displaystyle x_{2}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{u_{2,1}^{(\mu+3)}(z)-1}=\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{z^{4}-4\mu z^{3}+6\mu^{2}z^{2}-4\mu(\mu^{2}-1)z+\mu^{2}(\mu^{2}-1)z}{z^{2}-2(\mu+1)z+\mu(\mu-1)}.

Consequently, if we define the rational functions

\displaystyle x_{3n}(z)=X_{n}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{w_{1,n+1}^{(\mu-n-3)}(z)}{1-w_{1,n+1}^{(\mu-n-3)}(z)}=-1+\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{T_{0,n+2}^{(\mu-n-4)}(z)}{T_{0,n+1}^{(\mu-n-2)}(z)},

\displaystyle x_{3n+1}(z)=Y_{n}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{v_{1,n+1}^{(\mu+n)}(z)-1}=-1+\frac{\mu+n}{z}+\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{T_{0,n+2}^{(\mu-n-3)}(z)}{T_{1,n+1}^{(\mu-n-3)}(z)},

\displaystyle x_{3n+2}(z)=Z_{n}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{u_{n+2,1}^{(\mu+n+3)}(z)-1}=\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{T_{1,n+2}^{(\mu-n-4)}(z)}{T_{0,n+2}^{(\mu-n-4)}(z)},

then
x_{n}(z)
satisfies ternary d
P
I

x_{n}(x_{n+1}+x_{n-1}+1)+\frac{a_{n}}{z}=0,

with

a_{n}=\tfrac{1}{3}(n+\mu+5)+\tfrac{2}{3}(\mu-1)\cos\!\big(\tfrac{2}{3}\pi n\big)+\tfrac{4}{9}\sqrt{3}\,\sin\!\big(\tfrac{2}{3}\pi n\big),

whilst
X_{n}(z)
,
Y_{n}(z)
and
Z_{n}(z)
satisfy the discrete system

\displaystyle X_{n}(Y_{n}+Z_{n-1}+1)+\frac{n+\mu+1}{z}=0,

\displaystyle Y_{n}(Z_{n}+X_{n}+1)+\frac{n+3}{z}=0,

\displaystyle Z_{n}(X_{n+1}+Y_{n}+1)+\frac{n+2}{z}=0.

More generally, using the solution
w_{m,n}^{(\mu)}(z)
, for fixed
m
and
n
, as the initial solution then we have the following Lemma.

Lemma 5.12
.

Consider the rational functions

X_{N}(z)=\frac{w_{m,N+n}^{(\mu-N)}(z)}{1-w_{m,N+n}^{(\mu-N)}(z)},\quad Y_{N}(z)=\frac{1}{v_{m,N+n}^{(\mu+N+2n+1)}(z)-1},\quad Z_{N}(z)=\frac{1}{u_{N+n+1,m}^{(\mu+N+2m+2n+2)}(z)-1},

(5.10)

with
w_{m,n}^{(\mu)}(z)
,
v_{m,n}^{(\mu)}(z)
and
u_{m,n}^{(\mu)}(z)
as given in Theorem
4.4
,
then
X_{N}(z)
,
Y_{N}(z)
and
Z_{N}(z)
satisfy the discrete system

\displaystyle X_{N}(Y_{N}+Z_{N-1}+1)+\frac{N+m+2n+\mu+1}{z}=0,

(5.11a)

\displaystyle Y_{N}(Z_{N}+X_{N}+1)+\frac{N+m+n+1}{z}=0,

(5.11b)

\displaystyle Z_{N}(X_{N+1}+Y_{N}+1)+\frac{N+n+1}{z}=0,

(5.11c)

which is the system (
3.29
) with

a_{3N}=N+m+2n+\mu+1
,
a_{3N+1}=N+m+n+1
and
a_{3N+2}=N+n+1
, i.e. (
3.26a
) with

\lambda=\tfrac{1}{3}(m+2n+2\mu+1)
,
\rho=\tfrac{1}{3}(2m+4n+\mu+2)
and
\varphi=m+\tfrac{1}{3}
.

Proof.

From the transformations

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{3}\big(1/{w}_{m,n}^{(\mu)};m+2n+1+\mu,-m,-\mu\big)=\big(v_{m,n}^{(\mu+2n+1)};m+n+1,m+n+\mu+1,2n+1+\mu\big),

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{3}\big(v_{m,n}^{(\mu+2n+1)};m+n+1,m+n+\mu+1,2n+1+\mu\big)=\big(u_{n+1,m}^{(\mu+2m+2n+2)};n+1,-n-\mu,2m+2n+2+\mu\big),

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{3}\big(u_{n+1,m}^{(\mu+2m+2n+2)};n+1,-n-\mu,2m+2n+2+\mu\big)=\big(1/{w}_{m,n+1}^{(\mu-1)};m+2n+\mu+2,-m,\mu-1\big),

we obtain the sequence of solutions

\frac{1}{{w}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{3}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip v_{m,n}^{(\mu+2n+1)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{3}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip u_{n+1,m}^{(\mu+2m+2n+2)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{3}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\frac{1}{{w}_{m,n+1}^{(\mu-1)}}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{3}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip v_{m,n+1}^{(\mu+2n+2)}

from which the result follows. We note that

\mathcal{R}_{3}^{3}\Big(1/w_{m,n}^{(\mu)}\Big)=1/w_{m,n+1}^{(\mu-1)},\qquad\mathcal{R}_{3}^{3}\Big({v}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}\Big)={v}_{m,n+1}^{(\mu+1)},\qquad\mathcal{R}_{3}^{3}\Big({u}_{n,m}^{(\mu)}\Big)={u}_{n+1,m}^{(\mu+1)}.

∎

Lemma 5.13
.

Consider the rational functions

X_{N}(z)=\frac{1}{v_{m,N+n}^{(N+\mu)}(z)-1},\quad Y_{N}(z)=\frac{1}{u_{N+n+1,m}^{(N+\mu+2m+1)}(z)-1},\quad Z_{N}(z)=\frac{w_{m,N+n+1}^{(-N+\mu-2n-2)}(z)}{1-w_{m,N+n+1}^{(-N+\mu-2n-2)}(z)},

(5.12)

with
w_{m,n}^{(\mu)}(z)
,
v_{m,n}^{(\mu)}(z)
and
u_{m,n}^{(\mu)}(z)
as given in Theorem
4.4
,
then
X_{N}(z)
,
Y_{N}(z)
and
Z_{N}(z)
satisfy the discrete system

\displaystyle X_{N}(Y_{N}+Z_{N-1}+1)+\frac{N+m+n+1}{z}=0,

(5.13a)

\displaystyle Y_{N}(Z_{N}+X_{N}+1)+\frac{N+n+1}{z}=0,

(5.13b)

\displaystyle Z_{N}(X_{N+1}+Y_{N}+1)+\frac{N+m+\mu+1}{z}=0,

(5.13c)

which is the system (
3.29
) with

a_{3N}=N+m+n+1
,
a_{3N+1}=N+n+1
and
a_{3N+2}=N+m+\mu+1
, i.e. (
3.26a
) with

\lambda=\tfrac{1}{3}(m+n-\mu+1)
,
\rho=\tfrac{1}{3}(2m+2n+\mu+2)
and
\varphi=-m+n-\mu+\tfrac{1}{3}
.

Proof.

From the transformations

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{3}\big(v_{m,n}^{(\mu)};m+n+1,m-n+\mu,\mu\big)

\displaystyle=\big(u_{n+1,m}^{(\mu+2m+1)};n+1,n+1-\mu,2m+1+\mu\big),

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{3}\big(u_{m,n}^{(\mu)};m,m+2n+1-\mu,\mu\big)

\displaystyle=\big(1/{w}_{n,m}^{(\mu-2m-2n-1)};-n+\mu,-n,2m+2n+1-\mu\big),

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{3}\big(1/{w}_{m,n}^{(\mu)};m+2n+1+\mu,-m,-\mu\big)

\displaystyle=\big(v_{m,n}^{(\mu+2n+1)};m+n+1,m+n+\mu+1,2n+1+\mu\big),

we obtain the sequence of solutions

v_{m,n}^{(\mu)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{3}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip u_{n+1,m}^{(\mu+2m+1)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{3}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\frac{1}{{w}_{m,n+1}^{(\mu-2n-2)}}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{3}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip v_{m,n+1}^{(\mu+1)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{3}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip u_{n+2,m}^{(\mu+2m+2)}

from which the result follows.
We note that

\mathcal{R}_{3}^{3}\Big(1/w_{m,n}^{(\mu)}\Big)=1/w_{m,n+1}^{(\mu-1)},\qquad\mathcal{R}_{3}^{3}\Big({v}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}\Big)={v}_{m,n+1}^{(\mu+1)},\qquad\mathcal{R}_{3}^{3}\Big({u}_{n,m}^{(\mu)}\Big)={u}_{n+1,m}^{(\mu+1)}.

∎

Remark 5.14
.

There are no solutions in terms of generalised Laguerre polynomials in the symmetric case when
\lambda=\varphi=0
.

5.1.4
Partial difference systems

In this subsection we derive some partial difference systems which have solutions in terms of generalised Laguerre polynomials.

Lemma 5.15
.

If we define

\mathcal{W}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}=\frac{{w}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}+1}{{w}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}-1},\qquad\mathcal{V}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}=\frac{{v}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}+1}{{v}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}-1},\qquad\mathcal{U}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}=\frac{{u}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}+1}{{u}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}-1},

(5.14)

with
w_{m,n}^{(\mu)}(z)
,
v_{m,n}^{(\mu)}(z)
and
u_{m,n}^{(\mu)}(z)
as given in Theorem
4.4
,
then they satisfy the partial difference systems

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{m+1,n}^{(\mu)}+\mathcal{W}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}

\displaystyle+\frac{4}{z}\frac{(2m+2n+\mu+2)\mathcal{V}_{m,n}^{(\mu+2n+1)}-\mu}{1-\big(\mathcal{V}_{m,n}^{(\mu+2n+1)}\big)^{\!2}}=0,

(5.15a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{m,n}^{(\mu+2n+1)}+\mathcal{V}_{m-1,n}^{(\mu+2n+1)}

\displaystyle+\frac{4}{z}\frac{(2m+2n+\mu+1)\mathcal{W}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}-2n-\mu-1}{1-\big(\mathcal{W}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}\big)^{\!2}}=0,

(5.15b)

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{m,n+1}^{(\mu)}+\mathcal{V}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{z}\frac{(2n+2-\mu)\,\mathcal{U}_{n+1,m}^{(\mu+2m+1)}-\mu}{1-\big(\mathcal{U}_{n+1,m}^{(\mu+2m+1)}\big)^{\!2}},

(5.16a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{n+1,m}^{(\mu+2m+1)}+\mathcal{U}_{n,m}^{(\mu+2m+1)}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{z}\frac{(2n+1-\mu)\mathcal{V}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}+2m+\mu+1}{1-\big(\mathcal{V}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}\big)^{\!2}}.

(5.16b)

Proof.

These follow from setting
N=0
in (
5.6
) and (
5.7
), respectively.
∎

Lemma 5.16
.

If
\mathcal{W}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}
,
\mathcal{V}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}
and
\mathcal{U}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}
are given by (
5.14
), then they satisfy the partial difference systems

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{m+1,n}^{(\mu)}+\mathcal{W}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}

\displaystyle+\frac{4}{z}\frac{(2n+\mu+1)\,\mathcal{V}_{m,n}^{(\mu+2n+1)}-\mu}{1-\big(\mathcal{V}_{m,n}^{(\mu+2n+1)}\big)^{\!2}}=0,

(5.17a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{m,n}^{(\mu+2n+1)}+\mathcal{V}_{m-1,n}^{(\mu+2n+1)}

\displaystyle+\frac{4}{z}\frac{(2m+2n+\mu+1)\mathcal{W}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}-(2n+\mu+1)}{1-\big(\mathcal{W}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}\big)^{\!2}}=0,

(5.17b)

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{m+1,n-1}^{(\mu)}+\mathcal{W}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}

\displaystyle+\frac{4}{z}\frac{(2n+\mu)\,\mathcal{U}_{n,m}^{(\mu+2m+2n+1)}-\mu}{1-\big(\mathcal{U}_{n,m}^{(\mu+2m+2n+1)}\big)^{\!2}}=0,

(5.18a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{n+1,m-1}^{(\mu+2m+2n+1)}+\mathcal{U}_{n,m}^{(\mu+2m+2n+1)}

\displaystyle+\frac{4}{z}\frac{(2n+\mu+1)\mathcal{W}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}-(2m+2n+\mu+1)}{1-\big(\mathcal{W}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}\big)^{\!2}}=0.

(5.18b)

Proof.

From the transformations

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}\big(w_{m,n}^{(\mu)};-m,m+2n+\mu+1,\mu\big)

\displaystyle=\big(v_{m-1,n}^{(\mu+2n+1)};-m-n,m+n+\mu,\mu+2n+1\big),

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}\big(v_{m,n}^{(\mu)};-m-n-1,m-n+\mu,\mu\big)

\displaystyle=\big(w_{m,n}^{(\mu-2n-1)};-m,m+\mu,\mu-2n-1\big),

we obtain the sequence

w_{m,n}^{(\mu)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip v_{m-1,n}^{(\mu+2n+1)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip w_{m-1,n}^{(\mu)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip v_{m-2,n}^{(\mu+2n+1)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip w_{m-2,n}^{(\mu)}

from which the system (
5.17
) follows.

From the transformations

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}\big(w_{m,n}^{(\mu)};m,m+2n+\mu+1,\mu\big)

\displaystyle=\big(u_{n,m}^{(\mu+2m+2n+1)};-n,n+\mu,\mu+2m+2n+1\big),

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}\big(u_{m,n}^{(\mu)};-m,-m-2n-1+\mu,\mu\big)

\displaystyle=\big(w_{n+1,m-1}^{(\mu-2m-2n-1)};n+1,-n-1+\mu,\mu-2m-2n-1\big),

we obtain the sequence

w_{m,n}^{(\mu)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip u_{n,m}^{(\mu+2m+2n+1)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip w_{m+1,n-1}^{(\mu)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip u_{n-1,m+1}^{(\mu+2m+2n+1)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip w_{m+2,n-2}^{(\mu)}

from which the system (
5.18
) follows.
∎

Lemma 5.17
.

If
\mathcal{W}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}
,
\mathcal{V}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}
and
\mathcal{U}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}
are given by (
5.14
), then they satisfy the partial difference systems

\displaystyle\frac{m+\mu+1}{\mathcal{V}_{m,n}^{(\mu+1)}+\mathcal{U}_{n,m}^{(\mu+2m+1)}}+\frac{m+\mu}{\mathcal{V}_{m,n-1}^{(\mu-1)}+\mathcal{U}_{n,m}^{(\mu+2m+1)}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}z-\frac{\mu\,\mathcal{U}_{n,m}^{(\mu+2m+1)}+2n-\mu}{1-\big(\mathcal{U}_{n,m}^{(\mu+2m+1)}\big)^{\!2}},

(5.19a)

\displaystyle\frac{m+\mu}{\mathcal{V}_{m,n-1}^{(\mu-1)}+\mathcal{U}_{n,m}^{(\mu+2m+1)}}+\frac{m+\mu-1}{\mathcal{V}_{m,n-1}^{(\mu-1)}+\mathcal{U}_{n-1,m}^{(\mu+2m-1)}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}z-\frac{(2m+\mu)\mathcal{V}_{m,n-1}^{(\mu-1)}+2n-\mu}{1-\big(\mathcal{V}_{m,n-1}^{(\mu-1)}\big)^{\!2}},

(5.19b)

and

\displaystyle\frac{n+\mu-1}{\mathcal{W}_{m+1,n}^{(\mu-2)}+\mathcal{V}_{m,n}^{(\mu+2n)}}+\frac{n+\mu}{\mathcal{W}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}+\mathcal{V}_{m,n}^{(\mu+2n)}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}z-\frac{(\mu-1)\mathcal{V}_{m,n}^{(\mu+2n)}-(2m+2n+1+\mu)}{1-\big(\mathcal{V}_{m,n}^{(\mu+2n)}\big)^{\!2}},

(5.20a)

\displaystyle\frac{n+\mu}{\mathcal{W}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}+\mathcal{V}_{m,n}^{(\mu+2n)}}+\frac{n+\mu+1}{\mathcal{W}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}+\mathcal{V}_{m-1,n}^{(\mu+2n+2)}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}z-\frac{(\mu+2n+1)\mathcal{W}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}-(2m+2n+1+\mu)}{1-\big(\mathcal{W}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}\big)^{\!2}}.

(5.20b)

Proof.

These follow from setting
N=0
in (
5.8
) and (
5.9
), respectively.
∎

Lemma 5.18
.

Consider the rational functions

\mathcal{X}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}=\frac{w_{m,n}^{(\mu)}}{1-w_{m,n}^{(\mu)}},\qquad\mathcal{Y}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}=\frac{1}{v_{m,n}^{(\mu)}-1},\qquad\mathcal{Z}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}=\frac{1}{u_{m,n}^{(\mu)}-1},

with
w_{m,n}^{(\mu)}(z)
,
v_{m,n}^{(\mu)}(z)
and
u_{m,n}^{(\mu)}(z)
as given in Theorem
4.4
,
then they satisfy the partial difference system

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}\big(\mathcal{Y}_{m,n}^{(\mu+2n+1)}+\mathcal{Z}_{n,m}^{(\mu+2m+2n+1)}+1\big)+\frac{m+2n+\mu+1}{z}=0,

(5.21a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}_{m,n}^{(\mu+2n+1)}\big(\mathcal{Z}_{n+1,m}^{(\mu+2m+2n+2)}+\mathcal{X}_{m,n}^{(\mu)}+1\big)+\frac{m+n+1}{z}=0,

(5.21b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{n+1,m}^{(\mu+2m+2n+2)}\big(\mathcal{X}_{m,n+1}^{(\mu-1)}+\mathcal{Y}_{m,n}^{(\mu+2n+1)}+1\big)+\frac{n+1}{z}=0.

(5.21c)

Proof.

This follows from setting
N=0
in (
5.11
).
∎

5.2
Rational solutions in terms of generalised Umemura polynomials

5.2.1
First discrete equation: asymmetric discrete
P
II

In the following example, we illustrate how using the Bäcklund transformation
\mathcal{R}_{1}
(
3.3
) gives rise to a hierarchy of solutions of asymmetric d
P
II
(
3.7
) in terms of generalised Umemura polynomials.

Example 5.19
.

Applying the Bäcklund transformation
\mathcal{R}_{1}
with
(a,b,c)=(\kappa+\tfrac{1}{2},-\kappa+\tfrac{1}{2},2)
to the solution

{w}_{2,1}^{(\kappa-3/2)}(z)=-\frac{\big[z^{2}-16\kappa(\kappa+1)\big]\big[z^{3}-6(2\kappa-1)z^{2}+48\kappa(\kappa-1)z-32\kappa(\kappa-1)(2\kappa-1)\big]}{\big[z^{2}-16\kappa(\kappa-1)\big]\big[z^{3}-6(2\kappa+1)z^{2}+48\kappa(\kappa+1)z-32\kappa(\kappa+1)(2\kappa+1)\big]},

gives the sequence of rational solutions

{w}_{2,1}^{(\kappa-3/2)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip v_{2,1}^{(\kappa)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip w_{2,1}^{(\kappa-1/2)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip v_{2,1}^{(\kappa+1)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip w_{2,1}^{(\kappa+1/2)}.

Hence we obtain solutions of asymmetric d
P
II
(
3.7
)

\displaystyle q_{0}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{w_{2,1}^{(\kappa-3/2)}(z)+1}{w_{2,1}^{(\kappa-3/2)}(z)-1}=2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{z^{4}-8\kappa z^{3}+128\kappa(\kappa^{2}-1)z-256\kappa^{2}(\kappa^{2}-1)}{z-4\kappa},

\displaystyle q_{1}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{{v}_{2,1}^{(\kappa)}(z)+1}{{v}_{2,1}^{(\kappa)}(z)-1}=2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{z^{2}-16\kappa(\kappa+1)}{z^{3}-6(2\kappa+1)z^{2}+48\kappa(\kappa+1)z-32\kappa(\kappa+1)(2\kappa+1)},

\displaystyle q_{2}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{w_{2,1}^{(\kappa-1/2)}(z)+1}{w_{2,1}^{(\kappa-1/2)}(z)-1}=2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{z^{4}-8(\kappa+1)+128\kappa(\kappa+1)(\kappa+2)-256\kappa(\kappa+1)^{2}(\kappa+2)}{z-4(\kappa+1)},

\displaystyle q_{3}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{{v}_{2,1}^{(\kappa+1)}(z)+1}{{v}_{2,1}^{(\kappa+1)}(z)-1}=2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{z^{2}-16(\kappa+1)(\kappa+2)}{z^{3}-6(2\kappa+3)z^{2}+48(\kappa+1)(\kappa+2)z-32(\kappa+1)(\kappa+2)(2\kappa+3)}.

If we define the rational functions

\displaystyle q_{2n}(z)

\displaystyle=x_{n}(z)=\frac{w_{2,1}^{(\kappa+n-3/2)}(z)+1}{w_{2,1}^{(\kappa+n-3/2)}(z)-1}=2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{U_{2,1}^{(2\kappa+2n-3/2)}(z)}{U_{1,0}^{(2\kappa+2n+1/2)}(z)}

\displaystyle=2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{z^{4}-8(n+\kappa)z^{3}+128(n+\kappa)\big[(n+\kappa)^{2}-1\big]z-256(n+\kappa)^{2}\big[(n+\kappa)^{2}-1\big]}{z-4(n+\kappa)},

\displaystyle q_{2n+1}(z)

\displaystyle=y_{n}(z)=\frac{{v}_{2,1}^{(\kappa+n)}(z)+1}{{v}_{2,1}^{(\kappa+n)}(z)-1}=2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{U_{2,0}^{(2\kappa+2n+1/2)}(z)}{U_{1,1}^{(2\kappa+2n+1/2)}(z)}

\displaystyle=2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{z^{3}-6(2n+2\kappa+1)z^{2}+48(n+\kappa)(n+\kappa+1)-32(n+\kappa)(n+\kappa+1)(2n+2\kappa+1)}{z^{2}-16(n+\kappa)(n+\kappa+1)},

then
q_{n}(z)
satisfies

q_{n+1}+q_{n-1}=\frac{4}{z}\frac{(n+2\kappa)q_{n}+2-(-1)^{n}}{1-q_{n}^{2}},

which is asymmetric d
P
II
(
3.7
) with
\lambda=2\kappa
,
\rho=2
and
\varphi=-1
,
whilst
x_{n}(z)
and
y_{n}(z)
satisfy the discrete system

\displaystyle x_{n+1}+x_{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{z}\frac{(2n+2\kappa+1)y_{n}+3}{1-y_{n}^{2}},\qquad y_{n}+y_{n-1}=\frac{4}{z}\frac{2(n+\kappa)x_{n}+1}{1-x_{n}^{2}}.

More generally, using the solution
\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}
, for fixed
m
and
n
, then we have the following Lemma.

Lemma 5.20
.

Consider the rational functions

Q_{2N}(z)=X_{N}(z)=\frac{\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(N+\kappa)}(z)+1}{\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(N+\kappa)}(z)-1},\qquad Q_{2N+1}(z)=Y_{N}(z)=\frac{\widehat{v}_{m,n}^{(N+\kappa+n+1/2)}(z)+1}{\widehat{v}_{m,n}^{(N+\kappa+n+1/2)}(z)-1},

with
\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)
and
\widehat{v}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)
as given in Theorem
4.9
,
then
Q_{N}(z)
satisfies

Q_{N+1}+Q_{N-1}=\frac{4}{z}\frac{(N+m+n+2\kappa)Q_{N}+m-n(-1)^{N}}{1-Q_{N}^{2}},

which is asymmetric
d
P
II
(
3.7
) with
\lambda=m+n+2\kappa
,
\rho=m
and
\varphi=-n
,
whilst
X_{N}(z)
and
Y_{N}(z)
satisfy the discrete system

\displaystyle X_{N+1}+X_{N}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{z}\frac{(2N+m+n+2\kappa+1)Y_{N}+m+n}{1-Y_{N}^{2}},

(5.22a)

\displaystyle Y_{N}+Y_{N-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{z}\frac{(2N+m+n+2\kappa)X_{N}+m-n}{1-X_{N}^{2}}.

(5.22b)

Proof.

From the transformations

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}\big(\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)};m+\kappa,-n-\kappa,m+n\big)

\displaystyle=\big(\widehat{v}_{m,n}^{(\kappa+n+1/2)};m+n+\kappa+\tfrac{1}{2},-\kappa-\tfrac{1}{2},m-n\big),

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}\big(\widehat{v}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)};m+\kappa,n-\kappa,m-n\big)

\displaystyle=\big(\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa-n+1/2)};m-n+\kappa+\tfrac{1}{2},-\kappa-\tfrac{1}{2},m+n\big),

we obtain the sequence of solutions

\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{v}_{m,n}^{(\kappa+n+1/2)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa+1)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{v}_{m,n}^{(\kappa+n+3/2)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa+2)}

from which the result follows. We note that

\mathcal{R}_{1}^{2}\Big(\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\Big)=\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa+1)},\qquad\mathcal{R}_{1}^{2}\Big(\widehat{v}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\Big)=\widehat{v}_{m,n}^{(\kappa+1)},

so that only the parameter changes.
∎

Alternatively, using the solution
\widehat{v}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}
, for fixed
m
and
n
, then we have the following Lemma.

Lemma 5.21
.

Consider the rational functions

Q_{2N}(z)=X_{N}(z)=\frac{\widehat{v}_{N+m,N+n}^{(\kappa)}(z)+1}{\widehat{v}_{N+m,N+n}^{(\kappa)}(z)-1},\qquad Q_{2N+1}(z)=Y_{N}(z)=\frac{\widehat{u}_{N+m,N+n}^{(2\kappa-2N-2n)}(z)+1}{\widehat{u}_{N+m,N+n}^{(2\kappa-2N-2n)}(z)-1},

with
\widehat{v}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)
and
\widehat{u}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)
as given in Theorem
4.9
,
then
Q_{N}(z)
satisfies

Q_{N+1}+Q_{N-1}=\frac{4}{z}\frac{(N+m+n)Q_{N}+m-n+\kappa[1+(-1)^{N}]}{1-Q_{N}^{2}},

which is asymmetric
d
P
II
(
3.7
) with
\lambda=m+n
,
\rho=m-n+\kappa
and
\varphi=\kappa
,
whilst
X_{N}(z)
and
Y_{N}(z)
satisfy the discrete system

\displaystyle X_{N+1}+X_{N}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{z}\frac{(2N+m+n+1)Y_{N}+m-n}{1-Y_{N}^{2}},

(5.23a)

\displaystyle Y_{N}+Y_{N-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{z}\frac{(2N+m+n)X_{N}+2\kappa+m-n}{1-X_{N}^{2}}.

(5.23b)

Proof.

From the transformations

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}\big(\widehat{v}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)};m+\kappa,-n+\kappa,m-n\big)

\displaystyle=\big(\widehat{u}_{m,n}^{(2\kappa-2n)};m+\tfrac{1}{2},-n-\tfrac{1}{2},m-n+2\kappa\big),

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}\big(\widehat{u}_{m,n}^{(2\kappa)};m+\tfrac{1}{2},-n-\tfrac{1}{2},m+n+2\kappa\big)

\displaystyle=\big(\widehat{v}_{m+1,n+1}^{(n+\kappa)};m+n+\kappa+1,\kappa-1,m-n\big),

we obtain the sequence of solutions

\widehat{v}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{u}_{m,n}^{(2\kappa-2n)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{v}_{m+1,n+1}^{(\kappa)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{u}_{m+1,n+1}^{(2\kappa-2n-2)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{v}_{m+2,n+2}^{(\kappa)}

from which the result follows. We note that

\mathcal{R}_{1}^{2}\Big(\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\Big)=\widehat{w}_{m+1,n+1}^{(\kappa)},\qquad\mathcal{R}_{1}^{2}\Big(\widehat{u}_{m,n}^{(2\kappa-2n)}\Big)=\widehat{u}_{m+1,n+1}^{(2\kappa-2n-2)}.

∎

Remark 5.22
.

From the transformations

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}\big(\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)};m+\kappa,n+\kappa,m+n\big)

\displaystyle=\big(\widehat{u}_{m,n-1}^{(2\kappa+1)};m+\tfrac{1}{2},n-\tfrac{1}{2},m+n+2\kappa\big),

(5.24a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}\big(u_{m,n-1}^{(2\kappa+1)};m+\tfrac{1}{2},n-\tfrac{1}{2},m+n+2\kappa\big)

\displaystyle=\big(\widehat{w}_{m+1,n-1}^{(\kappa)};m+\kappa+1,n+\kappa-1,m+n\big),

(5.24b)

we obtain the sequence

\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{u}_{m,n-1}^{(2\kappa+1)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{w}_{m+1,n-1}^{(\kappa)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{u}_{m,n-2}^{(2\kappa+1)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{w}_{m+2,n-2}^{(\kappa)}.

It might be expected that the sequence would terminate when the second index becomes zero. However it can be shown that

\widehat{w}_{m,1}^{(\kappa)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{u}_{m,0}^{(2\kappa+1)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{w}_{m+1,0}^{(\kappa)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{u}_{m+1,0}^{(2\kappa)}

and

\widehat{u}_{m+1,0}^{(2\kappa)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{v}_{m+2,1}^{(\kappa)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{u}_{m+2,1}^{(2\kappa-2)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{v}_{m+3,2}^{(\kappa)}.

This is illustrated in the following example.

Example 5.23
.

Consider the rational solution

\widehat{w}_{1,1}^{(\kappa)}(z)=-\frac{(z-4\kappa-2)(z+4\kappa+6)}{(z+4\kappa+2)(z-4\kappa-6)},

which satisfies
P
V
with
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=\big(\tfrac{1}{2}(\kappa+1)^{2},\tfrac{1}{2}(\kappa+1)^{2},2\big)
, and so using (
5.24
) we obtain the sequence

\widehat{w}_{1,1}^{(\kappa)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{u}_{1,0}^{(2\kappa+1)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{w}_{2,0}^{(\kappa)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{u}_{2,0}^{(2\kappa)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{v}_{3,1}^{(\kappa)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip u_{3,1}^{(2\kappa-2)}.

The first few solutions are

\displaystyle q_{0}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\widehat{w}_{1,1}^{(\kappa)}(z)+1}{\widehat{w}_{1,1}^{(\kappa)}(z)-1}=\frac{4z}{z^{2}-4(2\kappa+1)(2\kappa+3)},

\displaystyle q_{1}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{4(\kappa+1)}{z}+2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{z-4\kappa-6}{z-4\kappa-2},

\displaystyle q_{2}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{6\big[z^{2}-8(\kappa+1)z+4(2\kappa+1)(2\kappa+3)\big]}{z^{3}-12(\kappa+1)z^{2}+12(2\kappa+1)(2\kappa+3)z-16(\kappa+1)(2\kappa+1)(2\kappa+3)}-\frac{2}{z-4\kappa-4},

\displaystyle q_{3}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{4(\kappa+1)}{z}+2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{z^{3}-6(2\kappa+3)z^{2}+48(\kappa+1)(\kappa+2)z-32(\kappa+1)(\kappa+2)(2\kappa+3)}{z^{3}-6(2\kappa+1)z^{2}+48\kappa(\kappa+1)z-32\kappa(\kappa+1)(2\kappa+1)},

and satisfy

q_{n+1}+q_{n-1}=\frac{4}{z}\frac{nq_{n}+2+[1+(-1)^{n}]\kappa}{1-q_{n}^{2}},

(5.25)

which is asymmetric d
P
II
(
3.7
) with
\lambda=0
,
\rho=2+\kappa
and
\varphi=\kappa
. In general

q_{2n}=x_{n}=2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{U_{n+1,n-2}^{(2\kappa-2n+3)}}{U_{n,n-1}^{(2\kappa-2n+3)}},\qquad q_{2n+1}=y_{n}=\frac{4(\kappa+1)}{z}+2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{U_{n+1,n-1}^{(2\kappa-2n+3)}}{U_{n+1,n-1}^{(2\kappa-2n+1)}},

satisfy (
5.25
) and

\displaystyle x_{n+1}+x_{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{z}\,\frac{(2n+1)y_{n}+2}{1-y_{n}^{2}},\qquad y_{n}+y_{n-1}=\frac{4}{z}\,\frac{2nx_{n}+2\kappa+2}{1-x_{n}^{2}}.

Symmetric case
.
In the symmetric case when
\varphi=0
in (
3.7
), then

q_{n}(z)=\frac{\widehat{w}_{m,0}^{(\kappa+n/2)}(z)+1}{\widehat{w}_{m,0}^{(\kappa+n/2)}(z)-1},\qquad p_{n}(z)=\frac{\widehat{w}_{0,m}^{(\kappa-n/2)}(z)+1}{\widehat{w}_{0,m}^{(\kappa-n/2)}(z)-1},

where
\widehat{w}_{m,0}^{(\kappa)}(z)
and
\widehat{w}_{0,m}^{(\kappa)}(z)
are given by (
4.20c
) and (
4.20d
) respectively,
which satisfy

\displaystyle q_{n+1}+q_{n-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{z}\frac{(n+m+2\kappa)q_{n}+m}{1-q_{n}^{2}},\qquad p_{n+1}+p_{n-1}=\frac{4}{z}\frac{(n-m-2\kappa)p_{n}+m}{1-p_{n}^{2}}.

5.2.2
Second discrete equation

In the following example, we illustrate how using the Bäcklund transformation
\mathcal{R}_{2}
(
3.13
) gives rise to a hierarchy of solutions of (
3.18
) in terms of generalised Umemura polynomials.

Example 5.24
.

Applying the Bäcklund transformation
\mathcal{R}_{2}
(
3.13
), with
(a,b,c)=(-\kappa-1,-\kappa-m,m+1)
to the solution
\widehat{w}_{1,m}^{(\kappa)}(z)
gives the sequence

\widehat{w}_{1,m}^{(\kappa)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{2}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{v}_{2,m}^{(\kappa+m-1/2)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{2}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{w}_{3,m}^{(\kappa-1)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{2}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{v}_{4,m}^{(\kappa+m-3/2)}

Hence if we define the rational functions

\displaystyle q_{2n}(z)

\displaystyle=x_{n}(z)=\frac{\widehat{w}_{2n+1,m}^{(\kappa-n)}(z)+1}{\widehat{w}_{2n+1,m}^{(\kappa-n)}(z)-1}=2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{U_{2n+1,m}^{(2\kappa-2n)}(z)}{U_{2n,m-1}^{(2\kappa-2n+2)}(z)},

\displaystyle q_{2n+1}(z)

\displaystyle=y_{n}(z)=\frac{\widehat{v}_{2n+2,m}^{(\kappa+m-n-1/2)}(z)+1}{\widehat{v}_{2n+2,m}^{(\kappa+m-n-1/2)}(z)-1}=2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{U_{2n+2,m-1}^{(2\kappa-2n-2m+2)}(z)}{U_{2n+1,m}^{(2\kappa-2n-2m+2)}(z)},

then
q_{n}(z)
satisfies

\frac{2n+3}{q_{n+1}+q_{n}}+\frac{2n+1}{q_{n}+q_{n-1}}=z-\frac{2\big[n+1-m(-1)^{n}\big]q_{n}+4\kappa+2m+2}{1-q_{n}^{2}},

which is equation (
3.18
) with
\lambda=m
,
\varphi=-n
and
\rho=m+n+2\kappa
,
whilst
x_{n}(z)
and
y_{n}(z)
satisfy the discrete system

\displaystyle\frac{4n+5}{x_{n+1}+y_{n}}+\frac{4n+3}{x_{n}+y_{n}}

\displaystyle=z-\frac{2(2n+m+1)y_{n}+4\kappa+2m+2}{1-y_{n}^{2}},

\displaystyle\frac{4n+3}{x_{n}+y_{n}}+\frac{4n+1}{x_{n}+y_{n-1}}

\displaystyle=z-\frac{2(2n-m+1)x_{n}+4\kappa+2m+2}{1-x_{n}^{2}}.

More generally, using the solution
\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}
, for fixed
m
and
n
, then we have the following Lemma.

Lemma 5.25
.

Consider the rational functions

Q_{2N}(z)=X_{N}(z)=\frac{\widehat{w}_{2N+m,n}^{(\kappa-N)}(z)+1}{\widehat{w}_{2N+m,n}^{(\kappa-N)}(z)-1},\qquad Q_{2N+1}(z)=Y_{N}(z)=\frac{\widehat{v}_{2N+m+1,n}^{(\kappa+n-N-1/2)}(z)+1}{\widehat{v}_{2N+m+1,n}^{(\kappa+n-N-1/2)}(z)-1},

with
\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)
and
\widehat{v}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)
as given in Theorem
4.9
,
then
Q_{N}(z)
satisfies

\frac{2N+2m+1}{Q_{N+1}+Q_{N}}+\frac{2N+2m-1}{Q_{N}+Q_{N-1}}=z-\frac{2\big[N+m-n(-1)^{N}\big]Q_{N}+2(m+n+2\kappa)}{1-Q_{N}^{2}},

which is equation (
3.18
) with
\lambda=m
,
\varphi=-n
and
\rho=m+n+2\kappa
,
whilst
X_{N}(z)
and
Y_{N}(z)
satisfy the discrete system

\displaystyle\frac{4N+2m+3}{X_{N+1}+Y_{N}}+\frac{4N+2m+1}{X_{N}+Y_{N}}

\displaystyle=z-\frac{2[(2N+m+n+1)Y_{N}+m+n+2\kappa]}{1-Y_{N}^{2}},

(5.26a)

\displaystyle\frac{4N+2m+1}{X_{N}+Y_{N}}+\frac{4N+2m-1}{X_{N}+Y_{N-1}}

\displaystyle=z-\frac{2[(2N+m-n)X_{N}+m+n+2\kappa]}{1-X_{N}^{2}}.

(5.26b)

Proof.

From the transformations

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{2}\big(\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)};-m-\kappa,-n-\kappa,m+n\big)

\displaystyle=\big(\widehat{v}_{m+1,n}^{(\kappa+n-1/2)};-m-n-\kappa-\tfrac{1}{2},-\kappa+\tfrac{1}{2},m-n+1\big),

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{2}\big(\widehat{v}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)};-m-\kappa,n-\kappa,m-n\big)

\displaystyle=\big(\widehat{w}_{m+1,n}^{(\kappa-n-1/2)};-m-\kappa-\tfrac{1}{2},n-\kappa+\tfrac{1}{2},m+n+1\big)

we obtain the sequence of solutions

\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{2}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{v}_{m+1,n}^{(\kappa+n-1/2)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{2}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{w}_{m+2,n}^{(\kappa-1)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{2}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{v}_{m+3,n}^{(\kappa+n-3/2)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{2}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{w}_{m+4,n}^{(\kappa-2)}

from which the result follows. We note that

\mathcal{R}_{2}^{2}\Big(\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\Big)=\widehat{w}_{m+2,n}^{(\kappa-1)},\qquad\mathcal{R}_{2}^{2}\Big(\widehat{v}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\Big)=\widehat{v}_{m+2,n}^{(\kappa-1)}.

∎

When
n=0
there is no alternating term and so

Q_{2N}(z)=\frac{\widehat{w}_{2N+m,0}^{(\kappa-N)}(z)+1}{\widehat{w}_{2N+m,0}^{(\kappa-N)}(z)-1},\qquad Q_{2N+1}(z)=\frac{\widehat{v}_{2N+m+1,0}^{(\kappa-N-1/2)}(z)+1}{\widehat{v}_{2N+m+1,0}^{(\kappa-N-1/2)}(z)-1},

satisfy

\frac{2N+2m+1}{Q_{N+1}+Q_{N}}+\frac{2N+2m-1}{Q_{N}+Q_{N-1}}=z-\frac{2(N+m)Q_{N}+2m+4\kappa}{1-Q_{N}^{2}}.

It is also possible to obtain a sequence of solutions involving the solutions

\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}
and
\widehat{u}_{m,n}^{(2\kappa+1)}
as illustrated in the following Lemma.

Lemma 5.26
.

Consider the rational functions

Q_{2N}(z)=X_{N}(z)=\frac{\widehat{w}_{N+m,N+n}^{(\kappa)}(z)+1}{\widehat{w}_{N+m,N+n}^{(\kappa)}(z)-1},\qquad Q_{2N+1}(z)=Y_{N}(z)=\frac{\widehat{u}_{N+m,N+n}^{(2\kappa+1)}(z)+1}{\widehat{u}_{N+m,N+n}^{(2\kappa+1)}(z)-1},

with
\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)
and
\widehat{u}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)
as given in Theorem
4.9
,
then
Q_{N}(z)
satisfies

\frac{2N+2m+2n+2\kappa+1}{Q_{N+1}+Q_{N}}+\frac{2N+2m+2n+2\kappa-1}{Q_{N}+Q_{N-1}}=z-\frac{2\big[N+m+n+\kappa\big\{1+(-1)^{N}\big\}\big]Q_{N}+2(m-n)}{1-Q_{N}^{2}},

which is equation (
3.18
) with
\lambda=m+n+\kappa
,
\varphi=\kappa
and
\rho=m-n
,
whilst
X_{N}(z)
and
Y_{N}(z)
satisfy the discrete system

\displaystyle\frac{4N+2m+2n+2\kappa+3}{X_{N+1}+Y_{N}}+\frac{4N+2m+2n+2\kappa+1}{X_{N}+Y_{N}}

\displaystyle=z-\frac{2[(2N+m+n+1)Y_{N}+m-n]}{1-Y_{N}^{2}},

(5.27a)

\displaystyle\frac{4N+2m+2n+2\kappa+1}{X_{N}+Y_{N}}+\frac{4N+2m+2n+2\kappa-1}{X_{N}+Y_{N-1}}

\displaystyle=z-\frac{2[(2N+m+n+2\kappa)X_{N}+m-n]}{1-X_{N}^{2}}.

(5.27b)

Proof.

From the transformations

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{2}\big(\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)};-m-\kappa,n+\kappa,m+n\big)

\displaystyle=\big(\widehat{u}_{m,n}^{(2\kappa+1)};-m-\tfrac{1}{2},n+\tfrac{1}{2},m+n+2\kappa+1\big),

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{2}\big(\widehat{u}_{m,n}^{(2\kappa+1)};-m-\tfrac{1}{2},n+\tfrac{1}{2},m+n+2\kappa+1\big)

\displaystyle=\big(\widehat{w}_{m+1,n+1}^{(\kappa)};-m-\kappa-1,n+\kappa+1,m+n+2\big).

we obtain the sequence of solutions

\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{2}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{u}_{m,n}^{(2\kappa+1)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{2}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{w}_{m+1,n+1}^{(\kappa)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{2}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{u}_{m+1,n+1}^{(2\kappa+1)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{2}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{w}_{m+2,n+2}^{(\kappa)}

from which the result follows. We note that

\mathcal{R}_{2}^{2}\Big(\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\Big)=\widehat{w}_{m+1,n+1}^{(\kappa)},\qquad\mathcal{R}_{2}^{2}\Big(\widehat{u}_{m,n}^{(2\kappa+1)}\Big)=\widehat{u}_{m+1,n+1}^{(2\kappa+1)}.

∎

When
\kappa=0
then there is no alternating term and so

Q_{2N}(z)=\frac{\widehat{w}_{N+m,N+n}^{(0)}(z)+1}{\widehat{w}_{N+m,N+n}^{(0)}(z)-1},\qquad Q_{2N+1}(z)=\frac{\widehat{u}_{N+m,N+n}^{(1)}(z)+1}{\widehat{u}_{N+m,N+n}^{(1)}(z)-1},

satisfy

\frac{2N+2m+2n+1}{Q_{N+1}+Q_{N}}+\frac{2N+2m+2n-1}{Q_{N}+Q_{N-1}}=z-\frac{2\big[(N+m+n)Q_{N}+m-n\big]}{1-Q_{N}^{2}},

which is equation (
3.18
) with
\lambda=m+n
,
\rho=m-n
and
\varphi=0
.
As previously, although there is no alternating term, the structure of the even and odd terms is different.

Symmetric case
.
In the symmetric case when
\varphi=0
in (
3.19
), from the transformations

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{2}\big(\widehat{w}_{m,0}^{(\kappa)};-m-\kappa,\kappa,m\big)

\displaystyle=\big(\widehat{w}_{m+1,0}^{(\kappa-1/2)};-m-\kappa-\tfrac{1}{2},-\kappa+\tfrac{1}{2},m+1\big),

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{2}\big(\widehat{w}_{0,n}^{(\kappa)};n,n+\tfrac{1}{2},n+\kappa\big)

\displaystyle=\big(\widehat{w}_{0,n+1}^{(\kappa-1/2)};\kappa-\tfrac{1}{2},n+\kappa+\tfrac{1}{2},n+1\big),

recall (
4.20c
) and (
4.20d
), we obtain the respective sequences

\begin{split}&\widehat{w}_{m,0}^{(\kappa)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{2}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{w}_{m+1,0}^{(\kappa-1/2)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{2}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{w}_{m+2,0}^{(\kappa-1)}\\
&\widehat{w}_{0,n}^{(\kappa)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{2}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{w}_{0,n+1}^{(\kappa-1/2)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{2}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{w}_{0,n+2}^{(\kappa-1)}\end{split}.

If we define

q_{n}(z)=\frac{\widehat{w}_{m+n,0}^{(\kappa-n/2)}(z)+1}{\widehat{w}_{m+n,0}^{(\kappa-n/2)}(z)-1},\qquad p_{n}(z)=\frac{\widehat{w}_{0,n+m}^{(\kappa-n/2)}(z)+1}{\widehat{w}_{0,n+m}^{(\kappa-n/2)}(z)-1},

then
{q}_{n}(z)
and
p_{n}(z)
satisfy

\displaystyle\frac{2n+2m+1}{q_{n+1}+q_{n}}+\frac{2n+2m-1}{q_{n}+q_{n-1}}

\displaystyle=z-\frac{2(n+m)q_{n}+2m+4\kappa}{1-q_{n}^{2}},

\displaystyle\frac{2n+2m+1}{p_{n+1}+p_{n}}+\frac{2n+2m-1}{p_{n}+p_{n-1}}

\displaystyle=z-\frac{2(n+m)p_{n}-2m-4\kappa}{1-p_{n}^{2}},

which are the discrete equation (
3.19
) with
\lambda=m
,
\rho=2\kappa+m
,
\varphi=0
and

\lambda=m
,
\rho=-2\kappa-m
,
\varphi=0
, respectively.

5.2.3
Third discrete equation: ternary discrete
P
I

In the following example, we illustrate how using the Bäcklund transformation
\mathcal{R}_{3}
(
3.24
) gives rise to a hierarchy of solutions of ternary d
P
I
(
3.28
) in terms of generalised Umemura polynomials.

Example 5.27
.

Applying the Bäcklund transformation
\mathcal{R}_{3}
(
3.24
), with
(a,b,c)=(\kappa+1,-\kappa-m,m+1)
to the solution
{w}_{1,m}^{(\kappa)}
gives the sequence of rational solutions

\widehat{w}_{1,m}^{(\kappa)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{3}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{v}_{1,m}^{(\kappa+m+1/2)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{3}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{u}_{1,m}^{(2\kappa+1)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{3}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{w}_{2,m}^{(\kappa)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{3}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{v}_{2,m}^{(\kappa+m+1/2)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{3}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{u}_{2,m}^{(2\kappa+1)}

so that

\mathcal{R}_{3}^{3}\Big(\widehat{w}_{1,m}^{(\kappa)}\Big)=\widehat{w}_{2,m}^{(\kappa)},\qquad\mathcal{R}_{3}^{3}\Big(\widehat{v}_{1,m}^{(\kappa+m+1/2)}\Big)=\widehat{v}_{2,m}^{(\kappa+m+1/2)},\qquad\mathcal{R}_{3}^{3}\Big(\widehat{u}_{1,m}^{(2\kappa+1)}\Big)=\widehat{u}_{2,m}^{(2\kappa+1)}.

Hence if we define the rational functions

\displaystyle x_{3n}(z)={X}_{n}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\widehat{w}_{n+1,m}^{(\kappa)}(z)-1}=-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{U_{n+1,m}^{(2\kappa)}(z)}{U_{n,m-1}^{(2\kappa+2)}(z)},

\displaystyle x_{3n+1}(z)={Y}_{n}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\widehat{v}_{n+1,m}^{(\kappa+m+1/2)}(z)-1}=-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{U_{n+1,m-1}^{(2\kappa+2)}(z)}{U_{n,m}^{(2\kappa+2)}(z)},

\displaystyle x_{3n+2}(z)={Z}_{n}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\widehat{u}_{n+1,m}^{(2\kappa+1)}(z)-1}=-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{n+m+2\kappa+2}{z}+\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{U_{n+1,m}^{(2\kappa+2)}(z)}{U_{n+1,m}^{(2\kappa)}(z)},

then
x_{n}(z)
satisfies ternary d
P
I

x_{n}(x_{n+1}+x_{n-1}+1)+\frac{a_{n}}{z}=0,

with

a_{n}=\tfrac{1}{3}(n+m+2\kappa)+1+\tfrac{1}{3}(\kappa-m)\cos\!\big(\tfrac{2}{3}\pi n\big)+\tfrac{1}{9}(3m+3\kappa+1)\sqrt{3}\sin\!\big(\tfrac{2}{3}\pi n\big),

so

a_{3n}=n+\kappa+1,\qquad a_{3n+1}=n+m+\kappa+\tfrac{3}{2},\qquad a_{3n+2}=n+\tfrac{3}{2},

whilst
{X}_{n}(z)
,
{Y}_{n}(z)
and
{Z}_{n}(z)
satisfy

\displaystyle{X}_{n}\big({Y}_{n}+{Z}_{n-1}+1\big)+\frac{n+\kappa+1}{z}=0,

\displaystyle{Y}_{n}\big({Z}_{n}+{X}_{n}+1\big)+\frac{n+m+\kappa+\tfrac{3}{2}}{z}=0,

\displaystyle Z_{n}\big({X}_{n+1}+{Y}_{n}+1\big)+\frac{n+\tfrac{3}{2}}{z}=0.

More generally, using the solution
\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}
, for fixed
m
and
n
, then we have the following Lemma.

Lemma 5.28
.

Consider the rational functions

{X}_{N}(z)=\frac{1}{\widehat{w}_{N+m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)-1},\qquad{Y}_{N}(z)=\frac{1}{\widehat{v}_{N+m,n}^{(\kappa+n+1/2)}(z)-1},\qquad{Z}_{N}(z)=\frac{1}{\widehat{u}_{N+m,n}^{(2\kappa+1)}(z)-1},

(5.28)

with
\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)
,
\widehat{v}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)
and
\widehat{u}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)
as given in Theorem
4.9
,
then
{X}_{N}(z)
,
{Y}_{N}(z)
and
{Z}_{N}(z)
satisfy

\displaystyle{X}_{N}\big({Y}_{N}+{Z}_{N-1}+1\big)+\frac{N+m+\kappa}{z}=0,

(5.29a)

\displaystyle{Y}_{N}\big({Z}_{N}+{X}_{N}+1\big)+\frac{N+m+n+\kappa+\tfrac{1}{2}}{z}=0,

(5.29b)

\displaystyle Z_{N}\big({X}_{N+1}+{Y}_{N}+1\big)+\frac{N+m+\tfrac{1}{2}}{z}=0,

(5.29c)

which is the system (
3.29
) with

\lambda=\tfrac{1}{3}(\kappa-n)
,
\varphi=n+\kappa+\tfrac{1}{3}
and
\rho=m+\tfrac{1}{3}n+\tfrac{2}{3}\kappa
.

Proof.

From the transformations

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{3}\big(\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)};m+\kappa,-n-\kappa,m+n\big)

\displaystyle=\big(\widehat{v}_{m,n}^{(\kappa+n+1/2)};m+n+\kappa+\tfrac{1}{2},\kappa+\tfrac{1}{2},m-n\big),

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{3}\big(\widehat{v}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)};m+\kappa,-n+\kappa,m-n\big)

\displaystyle=\big(\widehat{u}_{m,n}^{(2\kappa-2n)};m+n+\tfrac{1}{2},\tfrac{1}{2},m-n+2\kappa\big),

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{3}\big(\widehat{u}_{m,n}^{(2\kappa)};m+\tfrac{1}{2},n+\tfrac{1}{2},m+n+2\kappa\big)

\displaystyle=\big(\widehat{w}_{m+1,n}^{(\kappa-1/2)};m+\kappa+\tfrac{1}{2},-n-\kappa+\tfrac{1}{2},m+n+1\big),

we obtain the sequence of solutions

\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{3}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{v}_{m,n}^{(\kappa+n+1/2)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{3}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{u}_{m,n}^{(2\kappa+1)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{3}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{w}_{m+1,n}^{(\kappa)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{3}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{v}_{m+1,n}^{(\kappa+n+1/2)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{3}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{u}_{m+1,n}^{(2\kappa+1)}

from which the result follows. We note that

\mathcal{R}_{3}^{3}\Big(\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\Big)=\widehat{w}_{m+1,n}^{(\kappa)},\qquad\mathcal{R}_{3}^{3}\Big(\widehat{v}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\Big)=\widehat{v}_{m+1,n}^{(\kappa)},\qquad\mathcal{R}_{3}^{3}\Big(\widehat{u}_{m,n}^{(2\kappa+1)}\Big)=\widehat{u}_{m+1,n}^{(2\kappa+1)}.

∎

Remark 5.29
.

It is interesting to note that
{X}_{N}(z)
,
{Y}_{N}(z)
and
Z_{N}(z)
arise from the three different sets of rational solutions of
P
V
that are expressed in terms of the generalised Umemura polynomials.

As was the case for
\mathcal{R}_{2}
, it is also possible to obtain another sequence of solutions as illustrated in the following Lemma.

Lemma 5.30
.

Consider the rational functions

{X}_{N}(z)=\frac{1}{\widehat{w}_{N+m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)-1},\qquad{Y}_{N}(z)=\frac{1}{\widehat{u}_{N+m,n-1}^{(2\kappa+1)}(z)-1},\qquad{Z}_{N}(z)=\frac{1}{\widehat{v}_{N+m+1,n}^{(\kappa+n-1/2)}(z)-1},

(5.30)

with
\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)
,
\widehat{v}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)
and
\widehat{u}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)
as given in Theorem
4.9
,
then
{X}_{N}(z)
,
{Y}_{N}(z)
and
{Z}_{N}(z)
satisfy

\displaystyle{X}_{N}\big({Y}_{N}+{Z}_{N-1}+1\big)+\frac{N+m+\kappa}{z}=0,

(5.31a)

\displaystyle{Y}_{N}\big({Z}_{N}+{X}_{N}+1\big)+\frac{N+m+\tfrac{1}{2}}{z}=0,

(5.31b)

\displaystyle{Z}_{N}\big({X}_{N+1}+{Y}_{N}+1\big)+\frac{N+m+n+\kappa+\tfrac{1}{2}}{z}=0,

(5.31c)

which is the system (
3.29
) with

\lambda=\tfrac{1}{3}(\kappa-n)
,
\varphi=-n-\kappa+\tfrac{1}{3}
and
\rho=m+\tfrac{1}{3}n+\tfrac{2}{3}\kappa
.

Proof.

From the transformations

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{3}\big(\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)};m+\kappa,n+\kappa,m+n\big)

\displaystyle=\big(\widehat{u}_{m,n-1}^{(2\kappa+1)};m+\tfrac{1}{2},-n+\tfrac{1}{2},m+n+2\kappa\big),

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{3}\big(\widehat{u}_{m,n}^{(2\kappa)};m+\tfrac{1}{2},-n-\tfrac{1}{2},m+n+2\kappa\big)

\displaystyle=\big(\widehat{v}_{m+1,n+1}^{(\kappa+n)};m+n+\kappa+1,-\kappa+1,m-n\big),

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{3}\big(\widehat{v}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)};m+\kappa,n-\kappa,m+n\big)

\displaystyle=\big(\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa-n+1/2)};m+\kappa+\tfrac{1}{2},-n+\kappa+\tfrac{1}{2},m+n\big),

we obtain the sequence of solutions

\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{3}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{u}_{m,n-1}^{(2\kappa+1)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{3}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{v}_{m,n}^{(\kappa+n-1/2)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{3}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{w}_{m+1,n}^{(\kappa)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{3}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\widehat{u}_{m+1,n-1}^{(2\kappa+1)}

from which the result follows. We note that

\mathcal{R}_{3}^{3}\Big(\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\Big)=\widehat{w}_{m+1,n}^{(\kappa)},\qquad\mathcal{R}_{3}^{3}\Big(\widehat{v}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\Big)=\widehat{v}_{m+1,n}^{(\kappa)},\qquad\mathcal{R}_{3}^{3}\Big(\widehat{u}_{m,n}^{(2\kappa+1)}\Big)=\widehat{u}_{m+1,n}^{(2\kappa+1)}.

∎

Remark 5.31
.

There are no solutions in terms of generalised Umemura polynomials in the symmetric case when
\lambda=\varphi=0
.

5.2.4
Partial difference systems

In this subsection we derive some partial difference systems which have solutions in terms of generalised Umemura polynomials.

Lemma 5.32
.

If we define

\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)=\frac{\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)+1}{\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)-1},\qquad\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)=\frac{\widehat{v}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)+1}{\widehat{v}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)-1},\qquad\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)=\frac{\widehat{u}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)+1}{\widehat{u}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)-1},

(5.32)

with
\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)
,
\widehat{v}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)
and
\widehat{u}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)
as given in Theorem
4.9
,
then they satisfy the partial difference systems

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa+1)}+\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{z}\frac{(m+n+2\kappa+1)\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa+n+1/2)}+m+n}{1-\Big(\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa+n+1/2)}\Big)^{\!2}},

(5.33a)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa+n+1/2)}+\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa+n-1/2)}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{z}\frac{(m+n+2\kappa)\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}+m-n}{1-\Big(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\Big)^{\!2}},

(5.33b)

and

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa+n)}+\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{m+1,n+1}^{(\kappa+n)}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{z}\frac{(m+n+1)\,\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{m,n}^{(2\kappa)}+m-n}{1-\Big(\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{m,n}^{(2\kappa)}\Big)^{\!2}},

(5.34a)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{m,n}^{(2\kappa)}+\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{m+1,n+1}^{(2\kappa-2)}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{z}\frac{(m+n+2)\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{m+1,n+1}^{(\kappa+n)}+m+n+2\kappa}{1-\Big(\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{m+1,n+1}^{(\kappa+n)}\Big)^{\!2}}.

(5.34b)

Proof.

These follow from setting
N=0
in (
5.22
) and (
5.23
), respectively.
∎

Lemma 5.33
.

If
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)
and
\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)
are given by (
5.32
),

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{m+1,n}^{(\kappa)}+\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{m,n+1}^{(\kappa)}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{z}\frac{(m-n)\,\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{m,n}^{(2\kappa+1)}+m+n+1}{1-\Big(\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{m,n}^{(2\kappa+1)}\Big)^{\!2}},

(5.35a)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{m-1,n}^{(2\kappa+1)}+\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{m,n-1}^{(2\kappa+1)}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{z}\frac{(m-n)\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}+m+n+2\kappa}{1-\Big(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\Big)^{\!2}}.

(5.35b)

Proof.

From the transformations (
5.24
) we obtain the sequences

\begin{array}[]{ccccc}\widehat{w}_{m,n+1}^{(\kappa)}&\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}&\widehat{u}_{m,n}^{(2\kappa+1)}&\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}&\widehat{w}_{m+1,n}^{(\kappa)}\\
\widehat{u}_{m-1,n}^{(2\kappa+1)}&\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}&\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}&\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}&\widehat{u}_{m,n-1}^{(2\kappa+1)}\end{array}

from which the result follows. We note that

\mathcal{R}_{3}^{3}\Big(\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\Big)=\widehat{w}_{m+1,n}^{(\kappa)},\qquad\mathcal{R}_{3}^{3}\Big(\widehat{v}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\Big)=\widehat{v}_{m+1,n}^{(\kappa)},\qquad\mathcal{R}_{3}^{3}\Big(\widehat{u}_{m,n}^{(2\kappa+1)}\Big)=\widehat{u}_{m+1,n}^{(2\kappa+1)}.

∎

Lemma 5.34
.

If
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)
,
\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)
and
\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)
are given by (
5.32
), then they satisfy the partial difference systems

\displaystyle\frac{2m+1}{\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{m+1,n}^{(\kappa-1)}+\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa+n-1/2)}}+\frac{2m-1}{\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa)}+\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa+n-1/2)}}

\displaystyle=z-\frac{2\Big[(m+n)\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa+n-1/2)}+m+n+2\kappa-1\Big]}{1-\Big(\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa+n-1/2)}\Big)^{\!2}},

\displaystyle\frac{2m+1}{\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}+\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{m+1,n}^{(\kappa+n-1/2)}}+\frac{2m-1}{\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}+\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa+n+1/2)}}

\displaystyle=z-\frac{2\Big[(m-n)\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}+m+n+2\kappa\Big]}{1-\Big(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\Big)^{\!2}},

and

\displaystyle\frac{2m+2n+2\kappa+3}{\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{m+1,n+1}^{(\kappa)}+\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{m,n}^{(2\kappa+1)}}+\frac{2m+2n+2\kappa+1}{\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}+\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{m,n}^{(2\kappa+1)}}

\displaystyle=z-\frac{2\Big[(m+n+1)\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{m,n}^{(2\kappa+1)}+m-n\Big]}{1-\Big(\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{m,n}^{(2\kappa+1)}\Big)^{\!2}},

\displaystyle\frac{2m+2n+2\kappa+1}{\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}+\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{m,n}^{(2\kappa+1)}}+\frac{2m+2n+2\kappa-1}{\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}+\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{m-1,n-1}^{(2\kappa+1)}}

\displaystyle=z-\frac{2\Big[(m+n+2\kappa)\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}+m-n\Big]}{1-\Big(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\Big)^{\!2}}.

Proof.

These follow from setting
N=0
in (
5.26
) and (
5.27
), respectively.
∎

Lemma 5.35
.

If we define

\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)=\frac{1}{\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)-1},\qquad\widehat{\mathcal{Y}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)=\frac{1}{\widehat{v}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)-1},\qquad\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)=\frac{1}{\widehat{u}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)-1},

(5.36)

with
\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)
,
\widehat{v}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)
and
\widehat{u}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)
as given in Theorem
4.9
,
then they satisfy the partial difference system

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\Big(\widehat{\mathcal{Y}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa+n+1/2)}+\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{m-1,n}^{(2\kappa+1)}+1\Big)+\frac{m+\kappa}{z}=0,

(5.37a)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{Y}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa+n+1/2)}\Big(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{m,n}^{(2\kappa+1)}+\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}+1\Big)+\frac{m+n+\kappa+\tfrac{1}{2}}{z}=0,

(5.37b)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{m,n}^{(2\kappa+1)}\Big(\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{m+1,n}^{(\kappa)}+\widehat{\mathcal{Y}}_{m,n}^{(\kappa+n+1/2)}+1\Big)+\frac{m+\tfrac{1}{2}}{z}=0.

(5.37c)

Proof.

This follows from setting
N=0
in (
5.28
) and (
5.29
).
∎

5.3
Non-unique solutions of the discrete equations

Kitaev, Law and McLeod
[
46
, Theorem 1.2]
prove that if
\gamma\not\in\mathbb{Z}
in case (i), then the rational solution of
P
V
is unique and if
\gamma\in\mathbb{Z}
then there are at most two rational solutions. Clarkson and Dunning
[
16
]
gave examples of non-unique rational solutions which arise for solutions expressed in terms of generalised Laguerre polynomials discussed in §
5.1
. For example, the rational solutions

\displaystyle w_{1,1}^{(1)}(z)

\displaystyle=-\frac{(z-3)(z^{2}-8z+20)}{(z-2)(z-6)},\qquad u_{1,2}^{(1)}(z)=\frac{(z^{2}+4z+6)(z^{3}+9z^{2}+36z+60)}{z^{4}+12z^{3}+54z^{2}+96z+72},

(5.38)

both satisfy
P
V
(
1.2
) for the parameters
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=\big(\tfrac{1}{2},-\tfrac{25}{2},1\big)
. Hence the solutions

\displaystyle q(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{w_{1,1}^{(1)}(z)+1}{w_{1,1}^{(1)}(z)-1}=\frac{z^{3}-12z^{2}+52z-72}{(z-4)(z^{2}-6z+12)},

(5.39a)

\displaystyle p(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{u_{1,2}^{(1)}(z)+1}{u_{1,2}^{(1)}(z)-1}=\frac{z^{5}+14z^{4}+90z^{3}+312z^{2}+552z+432}{(z^{2}+6z+12)(z^{3}+6z^{2}+18z+24)},

(5.39b)

both satisfy
equation (
4.10
) for the parameters
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=\big(\tfrac{1}{2},-\tfrac{25}{2},1\big)
.
In this subsection, we use such pairs of solutions to derive examples of distinct hierarchies of rational solutions which satisfy the same discrete equation.

5.3.1
First discrete equation: asymmetric discrete
P
II

Applying the Bäcklund transformation
\mathcal{R}_{1}
(
3.3
), with
(a,b,c)=(1,-5,1)
, to the solutions (
5.38
) gives the sequences of rational solutions

\displaystyle w_{1,1}^{(1)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\frac{1}{{v}_{1,1}^{(4)}}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip w_{2,1}^{(1)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\frac{1}{{v}_{2,1}^{(4)}}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip w_{3,1}^{(1)}

\displaystyle u_{1,2}^{(1)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip{v}_{2,1}^{(4)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip u_{2,2}^{(1)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip v_{2,2}^{(4)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{1}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip u_{3,2}^{(1)}

Hence we obtain the hierarchies

\displaystyle q_{2n}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1+v_{n,1}^{(4)}(z)}{1-v_{n,1}^{(4)}(z)}=1-\frac{8}{z}+2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{T_{n,1}^{(1)}(z)}{T_{n-1,2}^{(1)}(z)},

(5.40a)

\displaystyle q_{2n+1}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{w_{n+1,1}^{(1)}(z)+1}{w_{n+1,1}^{(1)}(z)-1}=1+2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{T_{n,1}^{(2)}(z)}{T_{n,2}^{(0)}(z)},

(5.40b)

and

\displaystyle p_{2n}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{v_{2,n}^{(-4)}(z)+1}{v_{2,n}^{(-4)}(z)-1}=-1-\frac{8}{z}+2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{T_{1,n+1}^{(-2n-5)}(z)}{T_{2,n}^{(-2n-5)}(z)},

(5.41a)

\displaystyle p_{2n+1}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{u_{n+1,2}^{(1)}(z)+1}{u_{n+1,2}^{(1)}(z)-1}=1+2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{T_{2,n+1}^{(-2n-6)}(z)}{T_{1,n+1}^{(-2n-6)}(z)},

(5.41b)

where
q_{2n}
and
p_{2n}
both satisfy equation (
4.10
) for the parameters
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=\big(\tfrac{1}{2}(n+3)^{2},-\tfrac{1}{2}(n+2)^{2},-4\big)
whilst

q_{2n+1}
and
p_{2n+1}
both satisfy equation (
4.10
) for the parameters
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=\big(\tfrac{1}{2}(n+1)^{2},-\tfrac{1}{2}(n+5)^{2},1\big)
.
We note that there is a difference in the structure of the solutions in the hierarchies (
5.40
) and (
5.41
).

Both
q_{n}
and
p_{n}
satisfy the discrete equation

q_{n+1}+q_{n-1}=\frac{4}{z}\frac{(n+5)q_{n}-\tfrac{3}{2}+\frac{5}{2}(-1)^{n}}{1-q_{n}^{2}},

(5.42)

which is asymmetric d
P
II
(
3.7
) with
\lambda=5
,
\rho=-\tfrac{3}{2}
and
\varphi=\tfrac{5}{2}
.
Further, if either
(x_{n},y_{n})=(q_{2n},q_{2n+1})
or
(x_{n},y_{n})=(p_{2n},p_{2n+1})
then we obtain solutions to the discrete system

\displaystyle x_{n+1}+x_{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{z}\frac{(2n+6)y_{n}-4}{1-y_{n}^{2}},\qquad y_{n}+y_{n-1}=\frac{4}{z}\frac{(2n+5)x_{n}+1}{1-x_{n}^{2}}.

The first few solutions in the hierarchies (
5.40
) and (
5.41
) are given by

\displaystyle q_{0}

\displaystyle=1-\frac{8}{z}+2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln(z-3),

\displaystyle q_{2}

\displaystyle q_{4}

\displaystyle=1-\frac{8}{z}+2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{z^{3}-15z^{2}+60z-60}{z^{4}-20z^{3}+150z^{2}-480z+600},

\displaystyle q_{6}

\displaystyle=1-\frac{8}{z}+2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{z^{4}-24z^{3}+180z^{2}-480z+360}{z^{6}-36z^{5}+522z^{4}-3840z^{3}+15120z^{2}-30240z+25200},

\displaystyle q_{1}

\displaystyle=1+2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{z-4}{z^{2}-6z+12},

\displaystyle q_{3}

\displaystyle=1+2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{z^{2}-10z+20}{z^{4}-16z^{3}+96z^{2}-240z+240},

\displaystyle q_{5}

\displaystyle=1+2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{z^{3}-18z^{2}+90z-120}{z^{6}-30z^{5}+360z^{4}-2160z^{3}+6840z^{2}-10800z+7200},

\displaystyle q_{7}

\displaystyle=1+2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{z^{4}-28z^{3}+252z^{2}-840z+840}{z^{8}-48z^{7}+960z^{6}-10320z^{5}+64800z^{4}-241920z^{3}+524160z^{2}-604800z+302400},

and

\displaystyle p_{0}

\displaystyle p_{2}

\displaystyle=-1-\frac{8}{z}+2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{z^{6}+24z^{5}+228z^{4}+1056z^{3}+2520z^{2}+2880z+1440}{z^{6}+24z^{5}+252z^{4}+1440z^{3}+4680z^{2}+8640z+7200},
