with
q_{-1}(z)=p_{-1}(z)=1
.

5.3.2
Second discrete equation

The rational solutions

v_{0,n}^{(m+n+1)}(z)=(-1)^{n}\frac{m+1}{n+1}\,\frac{T_{0,n}^{(m-n+1)}(z)}{T_{0,n+1}^{(m-n-1)}(z)},\qquad\frac{1}{v_{m,0}^{(-m-n-1)}}(z)=-\frac{m+1}{n+1}\,\frac{T_{m,1}^{(-m-n-3)}(z)}{T_{m-1,1}^{(-m-n-3)}(z)},

both satisfy
P
V
(
1.2
) for the parameters
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=\big(\tfrac{1}{2}(n+1)^{2},-\tfrac{1}{2}(m+1)^{2},m+n+1\big)
. In the case when
m=n-1
, applying the Bäcklund transformation
\mathcal{R}_{2}
(
3.13
) gives the hierarchy of solutions

\displaystyle q_{2n}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1+v_{n,0}^{(-2n)}(z)}{1-v_{n,0}^{(-2n)}(z)}=1+\frac{4n}{z}-2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln T_{n-1,1}^{(-2n-1)}(z),

(5.43a)

\displaystyle q_{2n+1}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1+\widetilde{v}_{n,0}^{(-2n)}(z)}{1-\widetilde{v}_{n,0}^{(-2n)}(z)}=-1+2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln T_{n,1}^{(-2n-3)}(z),

(5.43b)

and

\displaystyle p_{2n}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{v_{0,n-1}^{(2n)}(z)+1}{v_{0,n-1}^{(2n)}(z)-1}=-1+\frac{4n}{z}-2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln T_{0,n-1}^{(1)}(z)

(5.44a)

\displaystyle p_{2n+1}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\widetilde{v}_{0,n-1}^{(2n)}(z)-1}{\widetilde{v}_{0,n-1}^{(2n)}(z)+1}=1+2\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln T_{0,n}^{(1)}(z),

(5.44b)

where

\displaystyle{v}_{n,0}^{(\mu)}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{n+\mu}{n+1}\frac{T_{n-1,1}^{(\mu-2)}(z)}{T_{n,1}^{(\mu-2)}(z)}=\frac{m+\mu}{m+1}\,\frac{L^{(\mu-1)}_{m}(z)}{L^{(\mu-1)}_{m+1}(z)},

\displaystyle\widetilde{v}_{n,0}^{(\mu)}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{T_{n,1}^{(\mu-2)}(z)}{T_{n-1,1}^{(\mu-2)}(z)}=\frac{L^{(\mu-1)}_{n+1}(z)}{L^{(\mu-1)}_{n}(z)},

\displaystyle{v}_{0,n}^{(\mu)}(z)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n}\frac{\mu-n}{n+1}\,\frac{T_{0,n}^{(\mu-2n)}(z)}{T_{0,n+1}^{(\mu-2n-2)}(z)}={\frac{n-\mu}{n+1}\,\frac{L_{n}^{(-\mu-1)}(-z)}{L_{n+1}^{(-\mu-1)}(-z)}},

\displaystyle\widetilde{v}_{0,n}^{(\mu)}(z)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n+1}\frac{T_{0,n+1}^{(\mu-2n-2)}(z)}{T_{0,n}^{(\mu-2n)}(z)}=\frac{L_{n+1}^{(-1-\mu)}(-z)}{L_{n}^{(-1-\mu)}(-z)}.

The solutions
q_{2n}
and
p_{2n}
both satisfy equation (
4.10
) for the parameters
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=\big(\tfrac{1}{2}n^{2},-\tfrac{1}{2}(n+1)^{2},2n\big)
whilst

q_{2n+1}
and
p_{2n+1}
both satisfy equation (
4.10
) for the parameters
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=\big(\tfrac{1}{2}n^{2},-\tfrac{1}{2}(n+1)^{2},2n+2\big)
. Again there is a difference in the structure of the solutions in the hierarchies (
5.43
) and (
5.44
).

Both
q_{n}
and
p_{n}
satisfy the discrete equation

\frac{2n+2}{q_{n+1}+q_{n}}+\frac{2n}{q_{n}+q_{n-1}}=z-\frac{\big[2n+1+(-1)^{n}\big]q_{n}-2}{1-q_{n}^{2}},

which is equation (
3.18
) with
\lambda=\tfrac{1}{2}
,
\rho=-2
and
\varphi=\tfrac{1}{2}
.
Further, if either
(x_{n},y_{n})=(q_{2n},q_{2n+1})
or
(x_{n},y_{n})=(p_{2n},p_{2n+1})
then we obtain solutions of the discrete system

\displaystyle\frac{2n+2}{x_{n+1}+y_{n}}+\frac{2n+1}{x_{n}+y_{n}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}z-\frac{(2n+1)y_{n}-1}{1-y_{n}^{2}},

\displaystyle\frac{2n+1}{x_{n}+y_{n}}+\frac{2n}{x_{n}+y_{n-1}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}z-\frac{(2n+1)x_{n}-1}{1-x_{n}^{2}}.

The first few solutions in the hierarchies (
5.43
) and (
5.44
) are given by

\begin{split}q_{2}&=1+\frac{4}{z}-\frac{2}{z+1},\\
q_{4}&=1+\frac{8}{z}-\frac{4(2+z)}{z^{2}+4z+6},\\
q_{6}&=1+\frac{12}{z}-\frac{6(z^{2}+6z+12)}{z^{3}+9z^{2}+36z+60},\\
q_{8}&=1+\frac{16}{z}-\frac{8(z^{3}+12z^{2}+60z+120)}{z^{4}+16z^{3}+120z^{2}+480z+840},\end{split}\qquad\begin{split}q_{1}&=-1+\frac{2}{z+1},\\
q_{3}&=-1+\frac{4(z+2)}{z^{2}+4z+6},\\
q_{5}&=-1+\frac{6(z^{2}+6z+12)}{z^{3}+9z^{2}+36z+60},\\
q_{7}&=-1+\frac{8(z^{3}+12z^{2}+60z+120)}{z^{4}+16z^{3}+120z^{2}+480z+840},\end{split}

and

\begin{split}p_{2}&=-1+\frac{4}{z},\\
p_{4}&=-1+\frac{8}{z}-\frac{2}{z-3},\\
p_{6}&=-1+\frac{12}{z}-\frac{4(z-4)}{z^{2}-8z+20},\\
p_{8}&=-1+\frac{16}{z}-\frac{6(z^{2}-10z+30)}{z^{3}-15z^{2}+90z-210},\end{split}\qquad\begin{split}p_{1}&=1,\\
p_{3}&=1+\frac{2}{z-3},\\
p_{5}&=1+\frac{4(z-4)}{z^{2}-8z+20},\\
p_{7}&=1+\frac{6(z^{2}-10z+30)}{z^{3}-15z^{2}+90z-210}.\end{split}

5.3.3
Third discrete equation: ternary discrete
P
I

The rational solutions

v_{3,1}^{(-1)}(z)=-\frac{(z-2)(z-6)}{(z-3)(z^{2}-8z+20)},\qquad v_{2,2}^{(-1)}(z)=-\frac{z^{4}+12z^{3}+54z^{2}+96z+72}{(z^{2}+4z+6)(z^{3}+9z^{2}+36z+60)},

(

(5.46a)

\displaystyle y(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{v_{2,2}^{(-1)}(z)-1}=-\displaystyle\frac{(z^{2}+4z+6)(z^{3}+9z^{2}+36z+60)}{(z^{2}+6z+12)(z^{3}+6z^{2}+18z+24)},

4.11
)
for the parameters

v_{3,1}^{(-1)}(z)={1/w_{1,1}^{(1)}(z)},\qquad v_{2,2}^{(-1)}(z)={1/u_{1,2}^{(1)}(z)}.

(

\displaystyle{v}_{3,1}^{(-1)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{3}\\[-8.0pt]

-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip\frac{1}{{w}_{3,2}^{(-6)}}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{3}\\[-8.0pt]

-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip u_{4,2}^{(6)}\enskip\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{R}_{3}\\[-8.0pt]
-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow\end{array}\enskip{v}_{2,4}^{(1)}

Hence we obtain the solution hierarchies

\displaystyle x_{3n}(z)=-1+\frac{n+3}{z}-\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln T_{0,n}^{(2-n)}(z)=-1+\frac{n+3}{z}-\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln L_{n}^{(-n-3)}(-z),

(5.47a)

\displaystyle x_{3n+1}(z)=-1+\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{T_{0,n+1}^{(1-n)}(z)}{T_{0,n}^{(3-n)}(z)}=-1+\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{L_{n+1}^{(-n-2)}(-z)}{L_{n}^{(-n-2)}(-z)},

(5.47b)

\displaystyle x_{3n+2}(z)=\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln T_{0,n+1}^{(2-n)}(z)=-1+\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{L_{n+1}^{(-n-2)}(-z)}{L_{n}^{(-n-2)}(-z)},

(5.47c)

and

\displaystyle y_{3n}(z)=\frac{n+3}{z}-\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln T_{1,1}^{(-n-4)}(z)=\frac{n+3}{z}-\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln L_{2}^{(-n-3)}(z),

(5.48a)

\displaystyle y_{3n+1}(z)=-1+\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{T_{1,1}^{(-n-5)}(z)}{T_{2,1}^{(-n-5)}(z)}=-1+\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{L_{2}^{(-n-4)}(z)}{L_{3}^{(-n-4)}(z)},

(5.48b)

\displaystyle y_{3n+2}(z)=-1+\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln T_{2,1}^{(-n-6)}(z)=-1+\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln L_{3}^{(-n-5)}(z),

(5.48c)

where
x_{n}
and
y_{n}
both satisfy equation (
4.11
) for the parameters
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=\big(\tfrac{1}{2}(n+1)^{2},-\tfrac{9}{2},n+3\big)
,

x_{3n+1}
and
y_{3n+1}
for the parameters
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=\big(\tfrac{1}{2}(n+4)^{2},-\tfrac{1}{2},n-2\big)
and

x_{3n+2}
and
y_{3n+2}
for the parameters
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=\big(\tfrac{1}{2}(n+1)^{2},-\tfrac{9}{2},n+5\big)
.
We remark that whilst
x_{n}
is only defined for
n\geq-1
,
y_{n}
is defined for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
. Also the solutions
x_{n}
only involve the generalised Laguerre polynomials
T_{0,n}^{(\mu)}(z)=(-1)^{\lceil n/2\rceil}L_{n}^{(-\mu-2n-1)}(-z)
,
whereas the solutions
y_{n}
only involve the generalised Laguerre polynomials
T_{1,1}^{(\mu)}(z)=L_{2}^{(\mu+1)}(z)
and
T_{2,1}^{(\mu)}(z)=L_{3}^{(\mu+1)}(z)
.
Both
x_{n}
and
y_{n}
satisfy the ternary d
P
I
equation

x_{n}(x_{n+1}+x_{n-1}+1)+\frac{a_{n}}{z}=0,

and the equation

\frac{a_{n+1}}{x_{n+2}+x_{n}+1}+\frac{a_{n-1}}{x_{n}+x_{n-2}+1}=z+\frac{a_{n}}{x_{n}},

with

a_{n}=\tfrac{1}{3}n+\tfrac{5}{3}-\tfrac{2}{3}\cos(\tfrac{2}{3}\pi n)+\tfrac{10}{9}\sqrt{3}\,\sin(\tfrac{2}{3}\pi n).

Further if
(X_{n},Y_{n},Z_{n})=(x_{3n},x_{3n+1},x_{3n+2})
or

(X_{n},Y_{n},Z_{n})=(y_{3n},y_{3n+1},y_{3n+2})
, then we obtain solutions of the discrete system

\displaystyle X_{n}\big(Y_{n}+Z_{n-1}+1\big)+\frac{n+1}{z}=0,\quad Y_{n}\big(Z_{n}+X_{n}+1\big)+\frac{n+4}{z}=0,\quad Z_{n}\big(X_{n+1}+Y_{n}+1\big)+\frac{n+1}{z}=0.

The first few solutions in the hierarchies (
5.47
) and (
5.48
) are given by

\begin{split}x_{0}&=-1+\frac{3}{z},\\
x_{3}&=-1+\frac{4}{z}-\frac{1}{z-3},\\
x_{6}&=-1+\frac{5}{z}-\frac{2(z-3)}{z^{2}-6z+12},\end{split}\qquad\begin{split}x_{1}&=-1+\frac{1}{z-3},\\
x_{4}&=-1+\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{z^{2}-6z+12}{z-4},\\
x_{7}&=-1+\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{z^{3}-9z^{2}+36z-60}{z^{2}-8z+20},\end{split}\qquad\begin{split}x_{2}&=\frac{1}{z-4},\\
x_{5}&=\frac{2(z-4)}{z^{2}-8z+20},\\
x_{8}&=\frac{3(z^{2}-8z+20)}{z^{3}-12z^{2}+60z-120},\end{split}

and

\begin{split}y_{0}&=\frac{3}{z}-\frac{2(z+1)}{z^{2}+2z+2},\\
y_{3}&=\frac{4}{z}-\frac{2(z+2)}{z^{2}+4z+6},\\
y_{6}&=\frac{5}{z}-\frac{2(z+3)}{z^{2}+6z+12},\end{split}\qquad\begin{split}y_{1}&=-1+\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{z^{2}+4z+6}{z^{3}+3z^{2}+6z+6},\\
y_{4}&=-1+\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{z^{2}+6z+12}{z^{3}+6z^{2}+18z+24},\\
y_{7}&=-1+\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\ln\frac{z^{2}+8z+20}{z^{3}+9z^{2}+36z+60},\end{split}\qquad\begin{split}y_{2}&=-1+\frac{3(z^{2}+4z+6)}{z^{3}+6z^{2}+18z+25},\\
y_{5}&=-1+\frac{3(z^{2}+6z+12)}{z^{3}+9z^{2}+36z+60},\\
y_{8}&=-1+\frac{3(z^{2}+8z+20)}{z^{3}+12z^{2}+60z+120}.\end{split}

6
Discussion

In this paper we have derived discrete equations from the Bäcklund transformations of
P
V
(
1.2
) and obtained hierarchies of rational solutions of the discrete equations.
Whilst the asymmetric d
P
II
(
3.7
), the discrete equation (
3.18
) and the ternary d
P
I
(
3.28
) were derived by
Tokihiro, Grammaticos and Ramani
[
78
]
from Bäcklund transformations of
P
V
(
1.2
), our approach gives the explicit relationship between solutions of the discrete equations and solutions of
P
V
. This enabled us to derive the hierarchies of rational solutions of the discrete equations.

Tokihiro, Grammaticos and Ramani
[
78
]
also derive the discrete system

x_{n}+x_{n-1}=\frac{1}{y_{n}}+\frac{n+\lambda+\mu}{1-y_{n}},\qquad y_{n+1}y_{n}=\frac{x_{n}-n-\lambda}{x_{n}^{2}-\varphi^{2}},

(6.1)

with
\lambda
,
\mu
and
\varphi
constants,
which was derived earlier by Fokas, Grammaticos and Ramani
[
23
]
from the Schlesinger transformations of
P
V
(
1.2
). Tokihiro, Grammaticos and Ramani
[
78
]
state that whilst
y_{n}
satisfies an equation which is equivalent to
P
V
,
x_{n}
satisfies a second-order, fourth-degree equation. Schlesinger transformations of
P
V
(
1.2
) are transformations which are quadratic in
\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}{w}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}
.
For example, consider the Schlesinger transformations
R_{1}
and
R_{2}
of
P
V
in
[
26
,
54
]
, which Fokas, Grammaticos and Ramani
[
23
]
use to derive the discrete system (
6.1
).
Suppose that
w_{0}=w(z;a_{0},b_{0},c_{0})
satisfies
P
V
for the parameters
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=\big(\tfrac{1}{2}a_{0}^{2},-\tfrac{1}{2}b_{0}^{2},c_{0}\big)
, then the Schlesinger transformations
R_{1}
and
R_{2}
are given by

\displaystyle w_{1}

\displaystyle=R_{1}(w_{0})=\frac{\big\{zw_{0}^{\prime}+a_{0}w_{0}^{2}-(a_{0}+b_{0}+z)w_{0}+b_{0}\big\}\big\{zw_{0}^{\prime}+a_{0}w_{0}^{2}-(a_{0}-b_{0}+z)w_{0}-b_{0}\big\}}{z^{2}\big(w_{0}^{\prime}\big)^{\!2}-2a_{0}zw_{0}(w_{0}-1)w_{0}^{\prime}+(a_{0}^{2}w_{0}^{2}-b_{0}^{2})(w_{0}-1)^{2}-2z(a_{0}-c_{0}+1)w_{0}(w_{0}-1)-z^{2}w_{0}^{2}},

\displaystyle w_{2}

\displaystyle=R_{2}(w_{0})=\frac{\big\{zw_{0}^{\prime}-a_{0}w_{0}^{2}+(a_{0}+b_{0}+z)w_{0}-b_{0}\big\}\big\{zw_{0}^{\prime}-a_{0}w_{0}^{2}+(a_{0}-b_{0}+z)w_{0}+b_{0}\big\}}{z^{2}\big(w_{0}^{\prime}\big)^{\!2}+2a_{0}zw_{0}(w_{0}-1)w_{0}^{\prime}+(a_{0}^{2}w_{0}^{2}-b_{0}^{2})(w_{0}-1)^{2}-2z(a_{0}-c_{0}-1)w_{0}(w_{0}-1)-z^{2}w_{0}^{2}},

with
{}^{\prime}=\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}{}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}
, and the effect on the parameters is

R_{1}\big(a_{0},b_{0},c_{0}\big)=\big(a_{0}+1,b_{0},c_{0}-1\big),\qquad R_{2}\big(a_{0},b_{0},c_{0}\big)=\big(a_{0}-1,b_{0},c_{0}+1\big).

Hence
w_{1}
and
w_{2}
respectively satisfy
P
V
(
1.2
) for the parameters
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=\big(\tfrac{1}{2}(a_{0}+1)^{2},-\tfrac{1}{2}b_{0}^{2},c_{0}-1\big)
and
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)=\big(\tfrac{1}{2}(a_{0}-1)^{2},-\tfrac{1}{2}b_{0}^{2},c_{0}+1\big)
, so that
R_{1}R_{2}=R_{2}R_{1}=\mathcal{I}
, the identity transformation. In terms of the Bäcklund transformations
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\varepsilon_{3}}
defined in §
2
, it is straightforward to show that

R_{1}\equiv\mathcal{T}_{1,1,1}^{2}\circ\mathcal{T}_{-1,-1,-1},\qquad R_{2}\equiv\mathcal{T}_{-1,-1,-1}^{2}\circ\mathcal{T}_{1,1,1}.

All the Schlesinger transformations of
P
V
(
1.2
) given in
[
26
,
54
]
can be written as a sequence of the Bäcklund transformations
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\varepsilon_{3}}
. The Schlesinger transformations in
[
26
,
54
]
are written in terms of the monodromy parameters
\theta_{0}
,
\theta_{1}
and
\theta_{\infty}
which are related to the parameters
a_{0}
,
b_{0}
and
c_{0}
through (
2.5
), i.e.

\theta_{0}=\tfrac{1}{2}(a_{0}+b_{0}-c_{0}-1),\qquad\theta_{1}=-\tfrac{1}{2}(a_{0}+b_{0}+c_{0}-1),\qquad\theta_{\infty}=a_{0}-b_{0},

so that

a_{0}=\tfrac{1}{2}(\theta_{0}-\theta_{1}+\theta_{\infty}),\qquad b_{0}=\tfrac{1}{2}(\theta_{0}-\theta_{1}-\theta_{\infty}),\qquad c_{0}=1-\theta_{0}-\theta_{1}.

Exact solutions of the discrete equations (
3.7
), (
3.18
) and (
3.28
) in terms of Kummer functions and modified Bessel functions, which are a special case of Kummer functions, cf.
[
62
, §13.6(iii)]
, using the explicit relationship between these discrete equations and
P
V
(
1.2
), is currently under investigation, and we do not pursue this further here.

Appendix A
Properties of the generalised Laguerre polynomials and the generalised Umemura polynomials

For convenience, in this appendix we list various differential-difference equations and discrete equations satisfied by the generalised Laguerre polynomials
T_{m,n}^{(\mu)}(z)
and the generalised Umemura polynomials
U_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)
.

A.1
Properties of the generalised Laguerre polynomials

Clarkson and Dunning
[
16
, §3]
prove the following differential-difference equations for the generalised Laguerre polynomials

\displaystyle{\rm D}_{z}\big(T_{m,n-1}^{(\mu+1)}{\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,}T_{m,n}^{(\mu)}\big)=T_{m+1,n-1}^{(\mu)}\,T_{m-1,n}^{(\mu+1)},

(A.1a)

\displaystyle{\rm D}_{z}\big(T_{m,n-1}^{(\mu+1)}{\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,}T_{m+1,n}^{(\mu-1)}\big)=T_{m+1,n-1}^{(\mu)}\,T_{m,n}^{(\mu)},

(A.1b)

\displaystyle{\rm D}_{z}\big(T_{m,n-1}^{(\mu+1)}{\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,}T_{m,n}^{(\mu-1)}\big)=T_{m+1,n-1}^{(\mu)}\,T_{m-1,n}^{(\mu)},

(A.1c)

\displaystyle{\rm D}_{z}\big(T_{m+1,n}^{(\mu)}{\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,}T_{m,n}^{(\mu+1)}\big)=T_{m+1,n-1}^{(\mu+1)}\,T_{m,n+1}^{(\mu)},

(A.1d)

\displaystyle{\rm D}_{z}\big(T_{m,n}^{(\mu)}{\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,}T_{m,n}^{(\mu+1)}\big)=T_{m+1,n-1}^{(\mu+1)}\,T_{m-1,n+1}^{(\mu)},

(A.1e)

\displaystyle{\rm D}_{z}\big(T_{m+1,n}^{(\mu)}{\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,}T_{m,n}^{(\mu)}\big)=T_{m+1,n-1}^{(\mu+1)}\,T_{m,n+1}^{(\mu-1)},

(A.1f)

where
{\rm D}_{z}(f{\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,}g)
is the Hirota operator (
4.15
),
and the discrete equations

\displaystyle T_{m,n+1}^{(\mu-1)}\,T_{m,n-1}^{(\mu+1)}

\displaystyle=T_{m+1,n}^{(\mu-1)}T_{m-1,n}^{(\mu+1)}-\big(T_{m,n}^{(\mu)}\big)^{\!2},

(A.2a)

\displaystyle{T_{m+1,n}^{(\mu)}\,T_{m,n}^{(\mu)}}

\displaystyle={T_{m+1,n}^{(\mu-1)}\,T_{m,n}^{(\mu+1)}}-T_{m,n+1}^{(\mu-1)}\,T_{m+1,n-1}^{(\mu+1)},

(A.2b)

\displaystyle T_{m,n}^{(\mu-1)}\,T_{m,n-1}^{(\mu+1)}

\displaystyle=T_{m,n}^{(\mu)}\,T_{m,n-1}^{(\mu)}-T_{m-1,n}^{(\mu)}\,T_{m+1,n-1}^{(\mu)}.

(A.2c)

The following differential-difference equations arise from the definitions of
w_{m,n}^{(\mu)}
,
v_{m,n}^{(\mu)}
and
u_{m,n}^{(\mu)}
in Theorem
4.4
and Remark
4.5

\displaystyle z{\rm D}_{z}\big(T_{m-1,n}^{(\mu)}{\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,}T_{m+1,n-1}^{(\mu)}\big)

\displaystyle=(m+1)T_{m,n-1}^{(\mu)}\,T_{m,n}^{(\mu)}+(z-m-2n-\mu)T_{m-1,n}^{(\mu)}T_{m+1,n-1}^{(\mu)},

(A.3a)

\displaystyle z{\rm D}_{z}\big(T_{m-1,n-1}^{(\mu+1)}{\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,}T_{m,n}^{(\mu-1)}\big)

\displaystyle=(m+n+\mu)T_{m,n-1}^{(\mu+1)}\,T_{m-1,n}^{(\mu-1)}-(m+n)T_{m,n}^{(\mu-1)}\,T_{m-1,n-1}^{(\mu+1)},

(A.3b)

\displaystyle z{\rm D}_{z}\big(T_{m,n-1}^{(\mu+1)}{\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,}T_{m-1,n+1}^{(\mu-1)}\big)

\displaystyle=nT_{m-1,n}^{(\mu+1)}\,T_{m,n}^{(\mu-1)}-(z-n-\mu)T_{m,n-1}^{(\mu+1)}\,T_{m-1,n+1}^{(\mu-1)},

(A.3c)

and so from (
A.1b
) and (
A.3b
)

zT_{m,n-1}^{(\mu)}\,T_{m-1,n}^{(\mu)}=(m+n+\mu)T_{m,n-1}^{(\mu+1)}\,T_{m-1,n}^{(\mu-1)}-(m+n)T_{m,n}^{(\mu-1)}\,T_{m-1,n-1}^{(\mu+1)}.

(A.4)

The generalised Laguerre polynomials
T_{m,n}^{(\mu)}(z)
satisfy the differential-difference equations

\displaystyle z{\rm D}_{z}^{2}\Big(T_{m,n}^{(\mu)}{\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,}T_{m,n}^{(\mu)}\Big)+2T_{m,n}^{(\mu)}\frac{\mathrm{d}{T_{m,n}^{(\mu)}}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}

\displaystyle=2(\mu+m+n+1)T_{m+1,n-1}^{(\mu+1)}T_{m-1,n+1}^{(\mu-1)},

(A.5a)

\displaystyle z{\rm D}_{z}^{2}\Big(T_{m,n}^{(\mu)}{\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,}T_{m,n}^{(\mu)}\Big)+2T_{m,n}^{(\mu)}\frac{\mathrm{d}{T_{m,n}^{(\mu)}}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}

\displaystyle=2(\mu+m+n+1)\left\{T_{m,n}^{(\mu+1)}T_{m,n}^{(\mu-1)}-\left(T_{m,n}^{(\mu)}\right)^{\!2}\right\},

(A.5b)

where

{\rm D}_{z}^{2}\big(f{\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,}g\big)=g\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}{f}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}^{2}}-2\frac{\mathrm{d}{f}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}\frac{\mathrm{d}{g}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}+f\frac{\mathrm{d}{g}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}},

from which we obtain the identity

T_{m+1,n-1}^{(\mu+1)}T_{m-1,n+1}^{(\mu-1)}=T_{m,n}^{(\mu+1)}T_{m,n}^{(\mu-1)}-\left(T_{m,n}^{(\mu)}\right)^{\!2}.

(A.6)

A.2
Properties of generalised Umemura polynomials

Masuda, Ohta and Kajiwara
[
53
]
, see also
[
52
]
, prove the following discrete equations for the generalised Umemura polynomials

\displaystyle U_{m+1,n}^{(\kappa-1)}\,U_{m-1,n+1}^{(\kappa+1)}

\displaystyle=U_{m+1,n+1}^{(\kappa-1)}\,U_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa+1)}+2(2m+1)\,U_{m,n+1}^{(\kappa-1)}\,U_{m,n}^{(\kappa+1)},

(A.7a)

\displaystyle U_{m,n+1}^{(\kappa-1)}\,U_{m+1,n-1}^{(\kappa+1)}

\displaystyle=U_{m+1,n+1}^{(\kappa-1)}\,U_{m,n-1}^{(\kappa+1)}-2(2n+1)\,U_{m+1,n}^{(\kappa-1)}\,U_{m,n}^{(\kappa+1)},

(A.7b)

\displaystyle U_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa)}\,U_{m+1,n-1}^{(\kappa)}

\displaystyle=z\,U_{m,n}^{(\kappa-2)}\,U_{m,n-1}^{(\kappa+2)}-2(2m+\kappa)\,U_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\,U_{m,n-1}^{(\kappa)},

(A.7c)

\displaystyle U_{m,n-1}^{(\kappa)}\,U_{m-1,n+1}^{(\kappa)}

\displaystyle=z\,U_{m,n}^{(\kappa-2)}\,U_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa+2)}+2(2n+\kappa)\,U_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\,U_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa)},

(A.7d)

\displaystyle U_{m+1,n}^{(\kappa-2)}\,U_{m-1,n-1}^{(\kappa+2)}

\displaystyle=z\,U_{m,n}^{(\kappa-2)}\,U_{m,n-1}^{(\kappa+2)}-2(\kappa-1)\,U_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\,U_{m,n-1}^{(\kappa)},

(A.7e)

\displaystyle U_{m,n+1}^{(\kappa-2)}\,U_{m-1,n-1}^{(\kappa+2)}

\displaystyle=z\,U_{m,n}^{(\kappa-2)}\,U_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa+2)}+2(\kappa-1)\,U_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\,U_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa)},

(A.7f)

\displaystyle U_{m-1,n+1}^{(\kappa-1)}\,U_{m,n-1}^{(\kappa+1)}

\displaystyle=z\,U_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa-1)}\,U_{m,n}^{(\kappa+1)}+2(2m+\kappa-1)\,U_{m,n}^{(\kappa-1)}\,U_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa+1)},

(A.7g)

\displaystyle U_{m+1,n-1}^{(\kappa-1)}\,U_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa+1)}

\displaystyle=z\,U_{m,n-1}^{(\kappa-1)}\,U_{m,n}^{(\kappa+1)}-2(2n+\kappa-1)\,U_{m,n}^{(\kappa-1)}\,U_{m,n-1}^{(\kappa+1)},

(A.7h)

\displaystyle U_{m+1,n}^{(\kappa-1)}\,U_{m-1,n-1}^{(\kappa+1)}

\displaystyle=z\,U_{m,n-1}^{(\kappa-1)}\,U_{m,n}^{(\kappa+1)}-2(2m+2n+\kappa)\,U_{m,n}^{(\kappa-1)}\,U_{m,n-1}^{(\kappa+1)},

(A.7i)

\displaystyle U_{m,n+1}^{(\kappa-1)}\,U_{m-1,n-1}^{(\kappa+1)}

\displaystyle=z\,U_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa-1)}\,U_{m,n}^{(\kappa+1)}+2(2m+2n+\kappa)\,U_{m,n}^{(\kappa-1)}\,U_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa+1)},

(A.7j)

where
{\rm D}_{z}(f{\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,}g)
is the Hirota operator (
4.15
),
see equation (4.16) and Proposition 4.1 in
[
53
]
.
From equations (A.12) and (A.13) in
[
52
, Appendix A]
one can derive the following differential-difference equations for the generalised Umemura polynomials

\displaystyle 2{\rm D}_{z}\big(U_{m,n-1}^{(\kappa)}{\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,}U_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa)}\big)

\displaystyle=U_{m,n-1}^{(\kappa)}\,U_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa)}-U_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\,U_{m-1,n-1}^{(\kappa)},

(A.8a)

\displaystyle 2{\rm D}_{z}\big(U_{m,n-1}^{(\kappa)}{\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,}U_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa)}\big)

\displaystyle=U_{m,n}^{(\kappa-2)}\,U_{m-1,n-1}^{(\kappa+2)}-U_{m,n-1}^{(\kappa)}\,U_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa)},

(A.8b)

\displaystyle 2{\rm D}_{z}\big(U_{m,n}^{(\kappa-1)}{\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,}U_{m-1,n-1}^{(\kappa+1)}\big)

\displaystyle=U_{m,n}^{(\kappa-1)}\,U_{m-1,n-1}^{(\kappa+1)}-U_{m,n-1}^{(\kappa+1)}\,U_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa-1)},

(A.8c)

\displaystyle 2{\rm D}_{z}\big(U_{m,n}^{(\kappa-1)}{\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,}U_{m-1,n-1}^{(\kappa+1)}\big)

\displaystyle=U_{m,n-1}^{(\kappa-1)}\,U_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa+1)}-U_{m,n}^{(\kappa-1)}\,U_{m-1,n-1}^{(\kappa+1)}.

(A.8d)

By adding and subtracting these equations we obtain the differential-difference equations

\displaystyle 4{\rm D}_{z}\big(U_{m,n-1}^{(\kappa)}{\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,}U_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa)}\big)

\displaystyle=U_{m,n}^{(\kappa-2)}\,U_{m-1,n-1}^{(\kappa+2)}-U_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\,U_{m-1,n-1}^{(\kappa)},

(A.9a)

\displaystyle 4{\rm D}_{z}\big(U_{m,n}^{(\kappa-1)}{\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,}U_{m-1,n-1}^{(\kappa+1)}\big)

\displaystyle=U_{m,n-1}^{(\kappa-1)}\,U_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa+1)}-U_{m,n-1}^{(\kappa+1)}\,U_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa-1)},

(A.9b)

and the discrete equations

\displaystyle 2U_{m,n-1}^{(\kappa)}\,U_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa)}

\displaystyle=U_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\,U_{m-1,n-1}^{(\kappa)}+U_{m,n}^{(\kappa-2)}\,U_{m-1,n-1}^{(\kappa+2)},

(A.10a)

\displaystyle 2U_{m,n}^{(\kappa-1)}\,U_{m-1,n-1}^{(\kappa+1)}

\displaystyle=U_{m,n-1}^{(\kappa+1)}\,U_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa-1)}+U_{m,n-1}^{(\kappa-1)}\,U_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa+1)}.

(A.10b)

Further differential-difference equations are given by

\displaystyle{\rm D}_{z}\big(U_{m+1,n}^{(\kappa-1)}{\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,}U_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa+1)}\big)

\displaystyle=(2m+1)U_{m,n}^{(\kappa-1)}\,U_{m,n}^{(\kappa+1)},

(A.11a)

\displaystyle{\rm D}_{z}\big(U_{m,n+1}^{(\kappa-1)}{\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,}U_{m,n-1}^{(\kappa+1)}\big)

\displaystyle=(2n+1)U_{m,n}^{(\kappa-1)}\,U_{m,n}^{(\kappa+1)}.

(A.11b)

The following differential-difference equations arise from the definitions of
\widehat{w}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}
,
\widehat{v}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}
and
\widehat{u}_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}
in Theorem
4.9
and Remark
4.10

\displaystyle z{\rm D}_{z}\big(U_{m-1,n}^{(2\kappa)}{\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,}U_{m,n-1}^{(2\kappa+2)}\big)

\displaystyle=(n+\kappa)U_{m-1,n}^{(2\kappa)}U_{m,n-1}^{(2\kappa+2)}-(m+\kappa)U_{m,n-1}^{(2\kappa)}\,U_{m-1,n}^{(2\kappa+2)},

(A.12a)

\displaystyle z{\rm D}_{z}\big(U_{m,n-1}^{(2\kappa)}{\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,}U_{m-1,n}^{(2\kappa+2)}\big)

\displaystyle=(m+\kappa)U_{m,n-1}^{(2\kappa)}U_{m-1,n}^{(2\kappa+2)}-(n+\kappa)U_{m-1,n}^{(2\kappa)}\,U_{m,n-1}^{(2\kappa+2)},

(A.12b)

\displaystyle z{\rm D}_{z}\big(U_{m-1,n-1}^{(2\kappa+1)}{\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,}U_{m,n}^{(2\kappa+1)}\big)

\displaystyle=\kappa U_{m-1,n-1}^{(2\kappa+1)}\,U_{m,n}^{(2\kappa+1)}-(m+n+\kappa)U_{m-1,n-1}^{(2\kappa+3)}\,U_{m,n}^{(2\kappa-1)},

(A.12c)

\displaystyle 2z{\rm D}_{z}\big(U_{m+1,n}^{(\kappa-1)}{\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,}U_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa+1)}\big)

\displaystyle=(2m+1)\left\{U_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa+1)}\,U_{m+1,n}^{(\kappa-1)}+U_{m,n-1}^{(\kappa+1)}\,U_{m,n+1}^{(\kappa-1)}\right\},

(A.12d)

\displaystyle 2z{\rm D}_{z}\big(U_{m,n+1}^{(\kappa-1)}{\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,}U_{m,n-1}^{(\kappa+1)}\big)

\displaystyle=(2n+1)\left\{U_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa+1)}\,U_{m+1,n}^{(\kappa-1)}+U_{m,n-1}^{(\kappa+1)}\,U_{m,n+1}^{(\kappa-1)}\right\}.

(A.12e)

Consequently from (
A.11a
) and (
A.12d
), or from (
A.11b
) and (
A.12e
), we obtain

2zU_{m,n}^{(\kappa-1)}\,U_{m,n}^{(\kappa+1)}=U_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa+1)}\,U_{m+1,n}^{(\kappa-1)}+U_{m,n-1}^{(\kappa+1)}\,U_{m,n+1}^{(\kappa-1)}.

(A.13)

The generalised Umemura polynomials
U_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}(z)
satisfy the differential-difference equations

\displaystyle U_{m+1,n}^{(\kappa)}\,U_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa)}

\displaystyle=4z{\rm D}_{z}^{2}\Big(U_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}{\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,}U_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\Big)+8U_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\frac{\mathrm{d}{U_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}+(z-6m-2n-2\kappa-2)\left(U_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\right)^{\!2},

(A.14a)

\displaystyle U_{m,n+1}^{(\kappa)}\,U_{m,n-1}^{(\kappa)}

\displaystyle=4z{\rm D}_{z}^{2}\Big(U_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}{\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,}U_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\Big)+8U_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\frac{\mathrm{d}{U_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}+(z+2m+6n+2\kappa+2)\left(U_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\right)^{\!2},

(A.14b)

and

\displaystyle U_{m+1,n}^{(\kappa-2)}\,U_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa+2)}

\displaystyle=4z{\rm D}_{z}^{2}\Big(U_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}{\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,}U_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\Big)+8U_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\frac{\mathrm{d}{U_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}+(z+2m-2n-2\kappa+2)\left(U_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\right)^{\!2},

(A.15a)

\displaystyle U_{m,n+1}^{(\kappa-2)}\,U_{m,n-1}^{(\kappa+2)}

\displaystyle=4z{\rm D}_{z}^{2}\Big(U_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}{\,\scriptscriptstyle\bullet\,}U_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\Big)+8U_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\frac{\mathrm{d}{U_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}}}{{\mathrm{d}{z}}}+(z+2m-2n+2\kappa-2)\left(U_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\right)^{\!2},

(A.15b)

with
U_{-1,-1}^{((\kappa))}(z)=U_{-1,0}^{((\kappa))}(z)=U_{0,-1}^{((\kappa))}(z)=U_{0,0}^{((\kappa))}(z)=1
.
Eliminating the derivative terms in (
A.14
) and (
A.15
) gives

\displaystyle U_{m,n+1}^{(\kappa)}\,U_{m,n-1}^{(\kappa)}-U_{m+1,n}^{(\kappa)}\,U_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa)}

\displaystyle=4(2m+2n+\kappa+1)\left(U_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\right)^{\!2},

(A.16a)

\displaystyle U_{m,n+1}^{(\kappa-2)}\,U_{m,n-1}^{(\kappa+2)}-U_{m+1,n}^{(\kappa-2)}\,U_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa+2)}

\displaystyle=4(\kappa-1)\left(U_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\right)^{\!2}.

(A.16b)

Also from (
A.14
) and (
A.15
) we obtain the difference equations

\displaystyle U_{m,n+1}^{(\kappa-2)}\,U_{m,n-1}^{(\kappa+2)}-U_{m+1,n}^{(\kappa)}\,U_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa)}

\displaystyle=4(2m+\kappa)\left(U_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\right)^{\!2},

(A.17a)

\displaystyle U_{m,n+1}^{(\kappa)}\,U_{m,n-1}^{(\kappa)}-U_{m+1,n}^{(\kappa-2)}\,U_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa+2)}

\displaystyle=4(2n+\kappa)\left(U_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\right)^{\!2},

(A.17b)

\displaystyle U_{m+1,n}^{(\kappa-2)}\,U_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa+2)}-U_{m+1,n}^{(\kappa)}\,U_{m-1,n}^{(\kappa)}

\displaystyle=4(2m+1)\left(U_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\right)^{\!2},

(A.17c)

\displaystyle U_{m,n+1}^{(\kappa)}\,U_{m,n-1}^{(\kappa)}-U_{m,n+1}^{(\kappa-2)}\,U_{m,n-1}^{(\kappa+2)}

\displaystyle=4(2n+1)\left(U_{m,n}^{(\kappa)}\right)^{\!2}.

(A.17d)
